# Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits)

> Authoring guidance: .claude/docs/templates/guidance/game-design-document-guide.md (load per-section as you author — do not read entirely).

> **Status**: Designed
> **Author**: user + level-designer, art-director, systems-designer, creative-director (via /design-system, autonomous; orchestrated run 2026-10-26)
> **Last Updated**: 2026-10-27
> **Last Verified**: 2026-10-27
> **Index position**: enumeration 14 / design order 14 (`systems-index.md`: Systems Enumeration row 14, Recommended Design Order row 14). Category Gameplay · Layer Feature · Priority MVP · Effort M. Specialists: level-designer (primary), art-director, systems-designer.
> **Implements Pillar**: P1 The World Never Lies (primary — every authored fact is a physical Graph subject, Layout rectangle or Item DB def: faults are Graph subjects, trails come only from Graph descriptors, lights are real emitters, geometry is one rect set, and no authored file carries a lie); P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total (primary, content side — salvage is the only faucet and this system owns the placement numbers and the part ledger: richness is placement density, part supply and authored `hp`, never def stats, and the MVP is always survivable with zero parts). **Serves**: P2 Dread Before Chaos (escape-route and tell-visibility content rules; no authored instakill without a tell); P3 The Web Watches (every authored lamp is a lantern the web can see; every room can be lit and un-lit); P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality (authored data is truth only; every false-able system has a world-visible witness in the same compartment).
> **Creative Director Review (CD-GDD-ALIGN)**: CONCERNS → REVISED 2026-10-26 (6 items applied)

## Summary

Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits) is the MVP's content layer: the hand-built data for the three ships the first build contains (the home-ship the player wakes in, a cold wreck and a hot wreck), the kit manifest they are assembled from, every placement table that fills them, and the content lint that holds it all honest; it owns authored files, not runtime state (no state block, hook, mutator or read view). Everything authored is a physical fact in an owner's schema (a Graph subject with a `def_id` and `hp`, a Layout rectangle, an Item DB def, an Emitter placement), so a wreck reads as a body with a cause of death, a usable organ and a cost of extraction, never as a level or a loot room. Read it to author or lint a ship, to see the eight faults, the part ledger and the numbers that make the first ten minutes work, or to learn the record and lint shapes Procedural Derelict Generation will assemble from later.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` — Priority: `MVP` — Key deps: `Ship Section & Layout Data Model` (designed), `Item & Module Database` (designed), `Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query` (designed); soft: Life-Support Graph, Failure & Trail, Interaction, Suit & Equipment, 2D Lighting (designed), Ship Body & Interior Physics (designed); downstream: Web Map & Transit, Boarding & Salvage (undesigned)

## Overview

Derelict Authoring (Handmade Kits) is the **content layer of the MVP**: the hand-built, data-only description of the three ships the player will ever see in the first build — the **home-ship** they wake in (the first derelict they learn to read), a cold wreck (**derelict A**) and a hot wreck (**derelict B**) — together with the **kit manifest** those ships are assembled from, every **placement table** that fills them (Graph nodes with `def_id` and `hp`, the eight live faults and their dried companions, lamps, the one beacon, loose parts and glowsticks, sockets, hardpoints, plate positions, spawn cells, the one visible unknown per wreck, and the reserved-but-empty story-slot list), and the **content lint** that holds all of it honest. It owns authored files, not runtime state: it registers no state block, no hook, no mutator and no read view (DA-1). Layout builds its files, the Graph instantiates its nodes, the Emitter instantiates its lights, Interaction places its loose items, and each of them owns its own schema, physics, vocabulary or light data. This GDD owns *what is authored* and the rules every authored file (and, later, every procedurally assembled one) must satisfy.

**How the player meets it.** They never meet "authoring"; they meet places, and the honesty of those places. The player wakes in the home-ship's cryo bay (one Tungsten lamp guttering, a coolant drip frosting a line across the floor to the far wall), closes a breaker, feels the room warm, follows the frost to the fitting that failed and patches it. They then ride a pod to a cold, black wreck whose only light is somebody else's lantern; they read dried trails by the chest lamp's beam alone, decide whether to feed a dead branch (and wake a machine that will start failing again, twice as fast as at home) or leave it dark, and carry a scrubber home. On a second sortie they cross a hot wreck with a scrammed reactor and a vented bay, and weld that wreck's dock onto their own ship. Every object they read, light, cut, carry or feed in the MVP is a record in this system's files; a playtester should be able to point at the weld seam afterwards and say which side was the original ship (Layout experience target X-1, answered here by the `_worn` kit, DA-5).

**What the game loses without it.** The Graph would have nothing to instantiate, the Item DB nothing to place, the weld nothing to join, and the eight MVP faults no home; the concept's MVP item 1 (authored places) would be unmet; and the Vertical Slice's Procedural Derelict Generation would have no kit vocabulary, record shapes or rule set to assemble (the kit definition is what procgen later assembles, so the manifest, the placement shapes and every lint are written as reusable data rules, not as one-off floor plans, DA-39 to DA-44).

**Index wording reconciled.** The index row calls for a "hand-built Foundry-dialect derelict and spawn ship" and says "MVP uses two handmade derelicts"; the concept's MVP item 1 reads "one handmade spawn derelict and one handmade target derelict, with an abstracted pod hop". Six designed GDDs (Life-Support Graph LS-26, Ship Section & Layout LY-25, Emitter State EM-24, Failure & Trail FT-38, 2D Lighting AC-LO-60, Save/Load SL-18) already size **three** sections, 14 compartments and eight faults. This GDD therefore authors **three ship templates**: `home_ship` (the spawn derelict, where the cryo bay is), `derelict_a` and `derelict_b` (the "two handmade derelicts" the player sorties to), plus a stub `pod` template for the transit leg (provisional, DA-3). Two pod hops; no pre-welded start. The concept and index wording ("one target", "a pod hop") are summaries and a wording refresh is routed, not edited (Cross-References).

| Scale at a glance (MVP, design counts) | `home_ship` | `derelict_a` | `derelict_b` | Total |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rooms (compartments) | 5 | 4 | 5 | 14 |
| Room volume `V` (tiles) | 182 | 118 | 149 | 449 (74.8 % of `TILES_WORLD_MVP` 600, the sum of all three; the largest legal loaded set is smaller, D.1) |
| Graph nodes | 42 | 27 | 35 | 104 (within the Graph's MVP bound of 120) |
| Hardpoints (module nodes + the home spare heater slot) | 27 | 18 | 22 | 67 |
| LOAD nodes (lamps, the beacon lamp, door motors, heaters) | 11 | 7 | 8 | 26 |
| Fixtures (lamp housings) | 8 | 6 | 7 | 21 |
| Live faults of the eight | 4 | 2 | 2 | 8 |
| Sockets | 1 | 0 | 1 | 2 (one mating pair) |
| Loose placements | 2 | 4 | 4 | 10 |
| Kit pieces (maintained + `_worn` + shared) | about 125 against the art bible's MVP sizing of about 110 (DA-38) | | | |
| Pod footnote (`pod`, DA-3) | 1 room, `V` 4, 0 fixtures, 0 nodes; +4 tiles and +1 body once, built at `init_world`; the pod section is always dormant (WM-5); not in the three-ship totals | | | |

**What this GDD is not.** It is not the template schema (Layout), the structural lint (Layout), the Graph's physics, the Item DB's vocabulary, the Emitter's light data, a pixel (the art-director and technical-artist draw), a procedural generator (VS-A), a story (no text, no names, no bodies), a combat encounter set (no authored entity placement) or a loot economy (no rarity, no rolls). Where this GDD touches an owner's rule it cites the rule id and never restates its value with a different number.

**Pillar alignment.** *Pillar 1 (primary), verbatim:* "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth. Only instruments and perception can be wrong." The authored places are where that sentence is made true or false: every cause of death in a wreck is a dried trail on a real Graph subject, every light has a source the player can walk to, and nothing authored is decoration pretending to be evidence. *Design test applied to content:* "a hidden stat vs. a visibly leaking pipe → the pipe": author the pipe. *Pillar 5 (primary, content side), verbatim:* "The outer ring is kept unsafe by depletion and wear (scrubbers degrade, salvage runs out), so there is no safe place to farm forever." This system owns the placement numbers of that sentence: the finite part ledger (5 parts against 7 full repairs, 714 permille, DA-25), the authored `hp` of every salvageable module, and G-0 (the MVP is survivable with zero parts). *Design test:* "safe farming vs. scarcity that pushes inward → scarcity." Pillars 2, 3 and 4 are served indirectly and named in the header; the anti-pillars are guard rails (Player Fantasy, DA-37).


## Player Fantasy

**Framing: the Forensic Salvager (direct).** *Read the dead ship, then decide what it costs to take its heart.* Derelict Authoring has no verb of its own and no screen; what the player touches is its output — three places and the honesty with which they were built — so the fantasy is a **reading fantasy with a price tag**, not a power fantasy. A dead ship is a sentence the chest lamp's beam can read: it tells you how it died, what it can spare and what it will cost you to take it, and it never lies, because it has no power left to lie with (art bible 2.6: dried trails "teach the loop grammar on a ship that cannot lie because it has no power to lie with"). Every place in the MVP was built so that one sweep of the beam answers three questions — who built this, what happened to it, has the web come in (art bible 6.0) — and then asks the only question the game cares about: *what will it cost to take it?* The concept's base is "a body they assembled from the corpses of other ships" (Core Fantasy): this system authors the corpses, and each must be read as a body with a cause of death, a usable organ and a cost of extraction, never as a level, a dungeon or a loot room.

(orchestrator ruling: three framings were weighed. A, "The Forensic Salvager" (direct, serves P1 and P5 co-primary), is adopted. B, "Somebody Else's Lantern" (the lure of the one lit thing on a dead ship) describes one beat per wreck and over-promises — a lure that promises a person and delivers a battery is a broken contract — so it is adopted only as the **first beat** of the anchor moment below. C, "The Organ Donor" (harvest) belongs to Modular Ship Assembly and would push flesh language into authored places, blurring the sane palette that P1 reserves for sanity leaks; "corpse" stays a concept-level metaphor and this GDD's own vocabulary stays mechanical.)

**Primary anchor moment: "You woke it. Now it is a clock." (derelict A, first sortie).**
1. *Transit.* From the pod, derelict A is black except one Tungsten point: the residual lamp `lamp_a_life` (DA-27) on a battery that runs down only while you are aboard. It is somebody else's lantern.
2. *Arrival.* Beam only (art bible 2.6). Dust in the cone. Under the closed door of the machine room a dried frost fan creeps across the sill — a dead coolant fault authored per DA-17. The eye follows trail to source to hatch (art bible 6.5).
3. *The lock is the physics.* Inside the cold room the branch is dead because a CABLE_JUNCTION sits at `hp` 2000 with a radial scorch at its box. There is no keycard. The spare `mod_breaker_junction` lies on a shelf in the life-support room, beside the residual lamp and the failed scrubber (the light points at the reason to board).
4. *The decision.* Swap the breaker and the dead branch wakes (DA-13 (c)): lanterns come on, which the web can see (P3), and a pump that was starved is now a live clock that runs at twice home speed (DA-13 (b)). Or leave it dark, cut the failed scrubber by beam alone (the first V8), and carry it home quiet. The decision is taken in the coldest room aboard, so feeding the wreck is also how a player survives staying (DA-28).
5. *The cost lands.* Waking the wreck was the player's act, so the faster failure that follows is their consequence, never the game's ambush.

The sentence we want overheard: *"If I power this, it starts dying again, and it'll be loud."* The rule behind it is stated once, in the player's terms, and is the only statement of derelict time behaviour that may appear in player-facing or design prose (it is the Graph's three mechanisms, DA-13): **a derelict does not degrade while you are away; a dead branch stays quiet until you feed it; once you are aboard and it is fed, it fails twice as fast as home.** The two secondary anchors are *warmth is spent, not owned* (the home-ship wake: the EMERGENCY lamp, the breaker, the room flooding with light and the heater's slow warm-up, about 6.5 minutes to COMFORT, D.18) and *which side was ours?* (the weld seam against the maintained and `_worn` kit). The playtest-checkable targets (X-DA-1 to X-DA-9; X-DA-8 is the anchor moment's own "leave it dark" test and X-DA-9 guards the treadmill) are written in Game Feel and Acceptance Criteria.

**Framing scope and the forward line (VS-B).** Co-op is outside MVP, so the Forensic Salvager is a solo fantasy here: one boarder reading the ship alone, and "we keep it alive minute by minute" is carried by *warmth is spent, not owned*. For Vertical Slice B (co-op) the same authored places need no schema change: a wreck's legibility (the dried trail on a real fitting, the dark lantern, the frost line) is exactly what a boarder reads aloud to the ship-keeper, so Fellowship (MDA rank 3) and the human channel (P4) build on these places as they stand.

**Pillar anchors, verbatim.** P1: "Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth." P5: "Risk and reward map to depth in the web. The outer ring is kept unsafe by depletion and wear ... so there is no safe place to farm forever." P2: "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived." P3: "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention ... shown in the world ... never as a hidden meter." P4: "No lie is unfalsifiable: the truth is always available through the physical world, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, the human channel, or time."

| Pillar | Rank here | What the player feels because of authoring |
|---|---|---|
| P1 The World Never Lies | PRIMARY | "I can read this." Every cause of death is a dried trail on a real fitting; every light has a source I can walk to; nothing in a wreck is decoration pretending to be a fault. |
| P5 Deeper Is Richer, Loss Is Not Total | PRIMARY | "This wreck can spare a few parts, not everything I need." The ledger is finite and visible as objects on the floor; choosing what to carry is the moment of value. |
| P2 Dread Before Chaos | SERVES | "I saw it coming and I had somewhere to run." The tell is visible across the room and a closable door is within reach (DA-29). |
| P3 The Web Watches | SERVES | "If I power this, it will be seen." Waking a branch lights lanterns the web sees; leaving it dark is a real strategy (every room can be lit and un-lit, DA-22). |
| P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality | SERVES (indirect) | "The gauge says fixed; the frost says otherwise." Authoring supplies the physical witnesses that make every MVP false-state lie falsifiable (DA-34). |

**Design test (this GDD's own).** Between an authored element the player is *told* about and one the player can *read* in one beam sweep and *verify* with one verb or one step, author the readable one; and if an authored element cannot be traced to a Graph subject, a Layout record or a `def_id`, it is not authored. Secondary test (P5): an extra part on the floor vs. a harder choice about which part to carry → the choice (the ledger sits at 714 permille inside the Item DB's advisory band, DA-25).

**Under hallucination the fantasy deepens, it does not break.** Authored data is truth only: the Perception Filter (undesigned) corrupts presentation at runtime, never the template, and Layout already rules out any phantom-geometry record (LY-34). A wreck is where truth is cheapest to check, because the instruments are dark and the player is already reading the world. The content rule that makes P4's "no lie is unfalsifiable" true is the **truth-witness rule** (DA-34): every authored subject that the Filter could misrepresent — a gauge, a breaker, a valve, a salvageable module, a live fault — has at least one consequence-channel witness in the same compartment (a trail live or dried, a frost line, a lantern on or off, a lit or dark fixture on its branch, the room's own temperature or pressure effect). MVP ships hallucination class (b), false system states; the other three classes are named so authoring does not foreclose them.

| P4 class | What authoring supplies so the lie is falsifiable | Worked example |
|---|---|---|
| (b) False system states (MVP) | the physical witness above; dead branches have dark fixtures, so "fixed" on a dark branch is checkable by looking at the lamp | A's pump reads "running" to a low-sanity player; the branch fixtures are dark and its lantern is off: the lie is visible in one sweep |
| (d) Phantom salvage (VS) | dried trails only where a dead fault was authored (FT:71's "cheap field read"); a finite ledger of real objects, none with violet or flesh pixels | a "pristine" scrubber with no trail in a room whose verdict says coolant failed: the absence of the authored trail is the field check before the carry home |
| (a) Phantom entities (VS) | nothing authored moves except periodic-motion media; dust, frost and door leaves a real entity's P1/P2 footprint can disturb | dust drifting in a sealed room is either the Tell or a lie; the frost line does not change for a lie |
| (c) Crewmates as horrors (VS) | a clear path of at least one tile, a 2 to 3 tile corridor, escape routes inside the fairness budget (DA-29) and a return path from every room: a sane player can always back away (a carrier at `V_CARRY` only after dropping cargo, Horror Entities C-HE-8); no authored one-way room where lethal friendly harm becomes unavoidable | a 3-tile corridor lets two crewmates pass without contact |

The residual light is a real emitter with a battery clock (DA-27), so it obeys P1; the exterior transit view must read the Emitter's state and the Filter may neither fabricate nor erase it there (routed ask to Perception Filter and Web Map & Transit, both undesigned).

**Reference techniques, adopted and rejected precisely.**

| Source | Technique | Verdict | Exactly what we take or refuse |
|---|---|---|---|
| Project Zomboid | "every door is a decision" | ADOPT | few doors per route, each a closable escape (DA-29); doors are the only authored pacing device; no scripted door events |
| Pandorum (first ten minutes) | waking into wrongness, a chest lamp as the only warmth | ADOPT | the cryo wake; the chest point is the beam origin for every authored reach and clearance (user decision); not its last act (no creature set pieces) |
| Return of the Obra Dinn | reconstructing a death from frozen physical evidence | ADOPT the method, REJECT the delivery | adopt: cause read from physical state (dried trail, door leaf state, pod state); reject: text, names, a deduction UI, a "solved" state |
| Subnautica (the Aurora) | a visible wreck that pulls you before you board | ADOPT the pull, REJECT the radio | adopt: one visible unknown per wreck from transit; reject: radio messages, objective markers, signal pings |
| Alien: Isolation | handmade, line-of-sight-led spaces where the environment is the tutorial | ADOPT | the cryo bay teaches through one carried element; the beacon has line of sight to the cryo-bay door (DA-24) |
| Dead Space | blood-writing, corpses, audio logs | REJECT | no bodies, no text, no logs in MVP; empty suits only, and those are Vertical Slice |
| Barotrauma | interlocking systems failing on a boarded ship | ADOPT the breach rhythm, REJECT freeform sub editing | rooms are pre-shaped kit assemblies; sockets are pre-declared |
| Diablo and looter-shooters | rarity tiers, loot rolls | REJECT | richness by placement density, part supply and authored `hp` only |
| Lock-and-key level design | key items gate progress | REJECT, REPLACE | the lock is the physics: a dead branch gated by a broken junction and a spare breaker that is a real module with ports |
| Ambush scripting | monster closets on trigger volumes | REJECT | no authored instakill without a tell; Attention and Horror own escalation; authoring places no entity |

**Anti-pillar guard rails (binding on every authored file; enforced in DA-37 and DA-40).** (1) *Not a freeform builder:* sections attach only at pre-declared sockets; modules are pre-shaped `def_id`s; the kit manifest is a content tool for authors and procgen, never a player palette. (2) *Not a combat game:* no authored enemy, spawn point, weapon cache, arena or turret; authoring leaves route data and light and power stimuli only. (3) *No intentional PvP:* no authored item whose only use is harming a crewmate, no armable trap, no reward placed where taking it requires another player to be exposed (review check). (4) *No authored linear story:* no chapters, cutscenes or scripted beats, ever; in MVP, story slots are `[]` and linted, and no text, names or bodies are placed. This is an MVP scope limit, not a permanent ban: the concept's anti-pillar is "not an authored linear story; lore is found and assembled", so Alpha lore fragments and the Narrative aesthetic stay open to the same kit schema, and what a later fragment may be is the narrative-director's to define. (5) *No win state:* no "wreck cleared", completion percentage or exhausted-wreck marker; a stripped wreck is a dark place with less in it. (6) *No meter:* no danger rating, salvage value, wreck tier, minimap or HUD icon for the visible unknown. (7) *Sane palette:* no flesh or violet in any kit piece or authored light. (8) *No fake evidence:* no loop-vocabulary decal that is not a Graph fault; no phantom, false or decoy record in any template. (9) *Dread before chaos:* every live fault has its endogenous tell; every exogenous hazard (B's breach) is entered by the player's own act; no authored one-way point (death is lethal including friendly fire, and MVP death is restart from the last autosave, which Save/Load owns). (10) *Restraint is possible:* every room can be dark; no forced-on authored light except the declared cryo EMERGENCY lamp and each wreck's residual lamp, both with physical sources.

**Words the GDD uses for authored content:** dead, dried, cold, dark, spare, residual, wake (a branch), feed (power or coolant), cut, carry, weep (a seam), somebody else's lantern. **Words it does not:** loot, treasure, drop, rarity, tier, level, dungeon, cleared, enemy, spawn point (for entities), secret, hidden room, puzzle, key, lock (as mechanics), "dormant until powered" (it conflates three Graph mechanisms, DA-13).


## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

Rule ids are `DA-n`. Lint ids are SCREAMING_SNAKE without a prefix (Layout's convention); `FIXTURE_COUNT_ROOM` and `FIXTURE_COUNT_WORLD` are used verbatim because Layout assigned them to this system. Formula ids `D.n` are this GDD's (Layout's are written `LY D.n`, the Graph's `LSG D.n`, and so on). Locked constants are used by registry name with their registry value; a number resting on a provisional upstream carries `[PLACEHOLDER: owner]` at every mention. Lines marked *(orchestrator ruling: ...)* record where the specialists disagreed and which reading this GDD adopts. **Formula index** (fixed here so the Formulas section does not renumber): D.1 tile volume budget per reachable set; D.2 count tolerance (one-sided); D.3 fixture bound use; D.4 part cover use and access order; D.5 escape-route use (run, walk, carry); D.6 residual-light clock, recharge and withdrawal; D.7 traverse bound use; D.8 containment colour count; D.9 PWR-OVL window, trigger and trunk cap; D.10 cold-room exposure budget; D.11 dead-fault back-date; D.12 fault-glow cascade bound; D.13 weld offset; D.14 leaky-kind wear clock; D.15 patch and fatigue treadmills; D.16 derelict A bus-on-battery window; D.17 reachable-set subject budget; D.18 thermal drift (the cold-room invariant); D.19 cryo-bay and ship air clocks; D.20 B's fatigue clock; D.21 B's restart reservoir floor.

#### A. Scope, roster, files, wear

**DA-1 Authoring is content, not runtime.** Derelict Authoring registers no state block (kernel CR-8), no step hook (CR-3), no mutator and no read_view. It owns the authored files, the kit manifest, the CONTENT lint and the ledgers. Its runtime consumers are Layout (`build_section`), the Graph (M1 `instantiate_section`), the Emitter (E1 `instantiate_section_emitters`) and Interaction (`place_loose`, `sync_section`), named in Interactions with Other Systems. Consequences: there is nothing to snapshot, no `schema_version` of its own and no kernel amendment; systems stay scene-tree free (kernel H10), so every lint and every content acceptance run is headless.

**DA-2 Roster: three ship templates, one role each.** (registry: `mvp_template_roster`) All three are `dialect: foundry`, `rot_q` 0 (no rotation, no mirror), `SECTION_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1. **Ring hint:** Authoring authors no ring hint in MVP; all four templates resolve to ring 1 by AT-6 (3) unless Web Map & Transit publishes a ring. A `ring_hint` field, if later wanted, is a Layout schema ask (new OQ-DA-29), never a content field minted here.

| `template_id` | Role | Kind | Rooms | Sockets | `entry_room_key` (the room the pod arrives in) | Sortie order (a recommendation, never a gate) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `home_ship` | the derelict the player wakes in (cryo bay); the base that grows | HOME | 5 | 1 (`sock_airlock_e`) | `airlock` | start |
| `derelict_a` | cold wreck; salvage only; never welded | DERELICT | 4 | 0 | `a_dock` | first sortie |
| `derelict_b` | hot wreck; the weld donor | DERELICT | 5 | 1 (`sock_b_w`) | `b_dock` | second sortie |

The index's "two handmade derelicts" are `derelict_a` and `derelict_b`; the spawn ship is `home_ship`. Derelict A yields a **module** (the failed scrubber at `hp` 2000, the first V8, with the spare breaker and the heater beside it), never a section; derelict B yields the **welded section** at `sock_b_w`. Derelict A is never welded, so it is only ever loaded beside a home body, never merged. Every built section starts on its own body and every multi-section body is produced by welds only (LY-25). The sortie order is an authored recommendation (A's salvage makes B's weld survivable, DA-25); freedom of sortie order is an anti-pillar-level promise (no scripted gate), and DA-32 therefore lints the post-weld sortie to A as a reachable loaded set. (orchestrator ruling: R1/R2 decide the roster and the donor; Layout LY-13 names B as the mate of the home-ship's socket three times against one clause in a Graph acceptance criterion; routed wording fixes are listed under CONFLICT headings in Cross-References.)

**DA-3 The pod stub and the spawn table (Ship Body & Interior Physics designed; provisional — Web Map & Transit undesigned).** Ship Body (BDY-25) rules that the pod hop is a body transfer and that **the pod is a Layout body built from a pod template**, and (BDY-20, Interactions row) that Authoring owns the spawn table converted by `init_world`. This GDD therefore authors a fourth, minimal template `pod` (one room `pod_cabin`, 2 x 2 tiles, `V` 4, no fixture, no module node, one seat cell, no loose slot; its Layout `kind`, whether it is exempt from `fixtures_min` 1 and whether `room_at` answers NONE on it are Layout's and Web Map & Transit's calls, OQ-DA-17). It adds 4 tiles to `D.1` once the persistent pod body exists (built at `init_world`, never per hop, section always dormant; registry `pod_body`) and counts one body against `MAX_BODIES` 8. The spawn table is data:

| `spawn_id` | Template, room | Section-local px (design target) | Consumed by | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `spawn_cryo_1` | `home_ship`, `cryo_bay` | (216, 168) | `init_world` wake, seat 1 | the player's open pod row; the cell is clear of `door_cryo_s`'s approach square and of the coolant floor run |
| `arrive_home_airlock` | `home_ship`, `airlock` | (552, 696) | Transit pod arrival | one tile clear east of the docked-podcraft prop |
| `arrive_a_dock` | `derelict_a`, `a_dock` | (168, 336) | Transit pod arrival | one tile clear of the docked-podcraft prop at the room's west wall |
| `arrive_b_dock` | `derelict_b`, `b_dock` | (168, 312) | Transit pod arrival | as above; after the weld this room is also the seam room |
| `seat_pod_1` | `pod`, `pod_cabin` | (72, 48) | Transit | Ship Body's illustrative cell |

Co-op seats 2 to 4 are Vertical Slice and are not authored (PlayerIds start at 1, KRN; seat 0 is Body's "no seat", BDY-7). Lint `SPAWN_BLOCKED` (ERROR, CI): each spawn passes Body's `can_place` (`BDY_PLACE_INSIDE_FOOTPRINT`) against Layout's rect set, the room is breathable at step 0, and no spawn lies within 1 tile of a fault's maximum floor run.

**DA-4 Files, ids and the world file.** Templates are `assets/data/sections/home_ship.json`, `derelict_a.json`, `derelict_b.json`, `pod.json`; the world file is `assets/data/world/mvp_world.json` (LY-25), listing `{template_id, kind, world_slot}` for each. `template_id` and `room_key` values are the stable strings in the tables below; renaming one after the first external build needs a migration step in the owner (LY-6 e, SL-14), and pre-release saves are disposable. Authoring adds to Layout's records only these CONTENT fields (nothing else; every other field is Layout's LY-4 and is cited by record kind, never restated): `def_id`, `hp`, `fail_since_step`, `forced_mode`, `switch_on`, `residual`, `condition`, `visible_unknown`, `socket_tag`, plus the initial values of the Graph's own persisted fields listed in the mapping table below. Those last are **not new schema** (guard rail G1): each is the authored initial value of one field the Graph already persists (LS-3, LS-21), named here by its owner field.

| Authored initial value (this GDD's shorthand) | Owner field it initialises | Unit and form | Used in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `charge` | BATTERY `stock` | integer dJ (never a percent; `BATT_CELL` 3,600,000 dJ is only the derivation base) | DA-19, DA-27, DA-35 |
| `valve_open` | VALVE edge `open` | bool | DA-35 |
| `stock` (air) | O2_TANK `stock` | integer mGU | DA-35 |
| `stock` (coolant, fuel) | RESERVOIR `stock_ml`, FUEL_TANK `stock_ml` | integer mL | DA-35 |
| `T0` | COMPARTMENT `T` | integer mC | C.1 to C.3 |
| `breaker_closed` | CABLE_JUNCTION `breaker_closed` | bool (already the Graph's name, LS-3) | DA-35 |
| `hp`, `forced_mode`, `fail_since_step` | node `hp`, `forced_mode`, `fail_since_step` | bp; enum; signed steps (OQ-DA-1) | DA-17, DA-19 |
| `switch_on` | Emitter FIXTURE `switch_on` (Emitter E1 placement field) | bool | DA-22 |
| `condition` | the loose item's `condition_bp` (Item DB; placed through Interaction's `place_loose`) | integer bp | DA-26 |

The shorthand words `charge`, `valve_open`, `stock` and `T0` are used in the room and state tables below; the table above is the one-to-one map, so no file carries a field the owners do not already define. Every authored number is an integer; `AUTHORED_NON_INTEGER` (ERROR) rejects a float, because the engine's JSON parser returns numbers as floats and the loader casts and bound-checks `[UNV: float behaviour in 4.7.2, spike]`.

**DA-5 Dialect and wear: the `_worn` kit.** All three ships use Foundry pieces. `home_ship` uses the maintained atlas; both wrecks use the `_worn` atlas (art bible 8.5 naming `<cat>[_<dialect>]_<subject>[_<variant tokens>]_<map>.png`). The PROJECTOR picks an atlas by `template_id`, so the slice names and `layout.json` are shared and **no Layout field carries wear** (LY:1099); a decal-overlay approach was rejected because it would need a record kind Layout lacks. `_worn` is a closed vocabulary (registry: `worn_variant_token`): **scuff-to-bare at rounded corners, dust (matte Graphite mid, never a third floor value), crevice darkening (Void Steel, one value step) and clogged grates**. It excludes scorch, soot, char, rust, warm stains and any stain of a loop's fluid, because those are Graph trails and a static decal of one is a loop-vocabulary decal that is not a Graph fault (FT-3, FT AC-44) and, for soot, a false hot-room history (P1). (orchestrator ruling: R23 listed "scorch/soot" in the wear vocabulary and the level-designer's draft repeated it; the art-director's V-1 narrowing is adopted, because the same draft's DA-32 and FT AC-44 forbid exactly that decal.) Hue-less tool scorch on a cut leaf or a cut hull piece is the crew's cutter, a kit state exempt from this rule (VA-8, DA-39). Kit wear (this rule, template-driven, never changes at runtime) and module condition (authored `hp`, changes at runtime) are two axes and must never be mixed: a worn wall around a pristine module and a dark failed module in a maintained room are both legal. This answers Layout OQ-25 at MVP; the art-director's acceptance gate is AC-V3 (Acceptance Criteria) and, if the art spike cannot meet it, X-1 is downgraded to Vertical Slice as LY:1099 allows rather than left unsupported. Lint `WEAR_TOKEN_MISMATCH` (ERROR, CI): a wreck template referencing a maintained piece, or `home_ship` referencing a `_worn` piece.

**DA-6 One meaning of `entry_room_key`.** It names the room the pod arrives in: `airlock`, `a_dock`, `b_dock`. The arrival is a presentation transition owned by Web Map & Transit (provisional); no pod hatch is a socket. Each of these rooms carries a docked-podcraft placeholder prop (kit gap K-3, DA-38). This GDD never calls derelict A "spawn".

#### B. Geometry: rooms, doors, sockets, hull, hardpoints

**DA-7 Room sizing by role.** Rooms are sized to the art bible's density table and to Layout's bounds, not to the Lighting C3 placeholder (DA-31). Every room: `V` within 4 to 200 tiles, each section within `SECTION_MAX_TILES_W` 48 x `SECTION_MAX_TILES_H` 32 and at most `MAX_ROOMS_PER_SECTION` 8 rooms (the worst section here has 5). Corridors are 3 tiles across (144 px, the `CORRIDOR_HEAD_NARROW` head clearance, so no corridor anchor can fail that rule). Machine rooms are at most 5 tiles deep (PIPE:874). A room deeper than `ROOM_DEPTH_SOFT_MAX` 5 or wider than `ROOM_WIDTH_SOFT_MAX` 12 carries `frame_exception` true (Layout's `ROOM_EXCEEDS_FRAME` is a WARN silenced by it): `cryo_bay` (depth 6), `spine_corridor` (13 wide), `cargo_bay` (depth 6), `a_spine` (11 deep) and `b_spine` (13 deep), five exceptions logged in the room tables. Every wall-flush kit piece has face height at most 72 px (PIPE:1068; `SOUTH_BAND_OCCUPIED` is Layout's WARN, cited). `exposed_tiles` values in the room tables are **design targets** computed by the convention of Layout's cryo fixture (one count per hull side, so a corner tile counts on both sides); the converter's D.4 value governs across 24 px lattice jogs (`EXPOSED_DRIFT` WARN, OQ-DA-13).

**DA-8 Door grammar: every door fits LY D.18 by construction.** A door assembly (frame plus hinge-side pocket) is 192 px on a straight wall run plus `DOOR_CORNER_MARGIN_PX` 48 on the non-hinge side, so a wall carrying a door offers a straight run of at least 240 px (5 tiles). Authored consequences: (a) no door on the 3-tile end of a corridor, doors sit on corridor long sides; (b) every room hosting a door shares at least 5 tiles of straight wall with its neighbour; (c) every hatch room has a 5-tile straight hull run; (d) with hinge LOW (interior doors and socket hatches alike; `HATCH_HINGE_AUTHORED` is an error), the door assembly occupies [`doorway_start_px` - 96, `doorway_start_px` + 96) and leaves at least `DOOR_CORNER_MARGIN_PX` 48 of straight run beyond its non-hinge end (LY D.18). Every door has `width_px` 96 and `area_max` 64 (the default; range 32 to 128). Door initial state is CLOSED except where a room table says OPEN. Door leaf art states used in MVP are **closed and open only**: the cut and welded leaf states stay in the kit for the socket seam and the Vertical Slice, because welded is a socket-seam state (LY-15) and cut is a verb result with no MVP Interaction verb; a leaf that looks welded yet opens is the world lying. (orchestrator ruling: R18/R23 (iv) allowed "closed/open/cut/welded" interior states and "welded shut from inside" as a verdict; the level-designer's narrowing is adopted, supported by the creative-director and art-director, and the "welded shut from inside" kit gap (iv) has no MVP piece.)

**DA-9 Topology is a tree; a return path always exists.** Each template's room adjacency through doors is a tree (cycle count 0; `ROOM_GRAPH_CYCLE` WARN), because one authored retreat per room is legible (P2) and the Graph rejects power-wiring cycles in MVP (LSG OQ 11). From every room there is a door path to `entry_room_key` treating doors as traversable (Layout's `ROOM_UNREACHABLE` covers reachability; `RETURN_PATH_TRAVERSE` covers the vacuum- or cold-bounded return, DA-28).

**DA-10 Sockets, lanes and the weld offset.** Exactly one socket on `home_ship`, one on `derelict_b`, none on `derelict_a`; `MAX_SOCKETS_PER_SECTION` is 4 and `SOCKET_SPACING_MIN_TILES` 2 is moot. Both sockets carry `class_id` 1 (`SOCKET_CLASS`, `DOCK_STD`), `socket_tag` `dock_std` (an authoring convenience; port types are authoritative, IDB:169), `width_tiles` 2, **lane 0 `{POWER, lane_index 0}`, lane 1 `{O2, lane_index 0}`** and one hatch (Modular Ship Assembly, on disk and In Design, repeats the lane set and the offset in MSA-11; `socket_plan` and `apply` are Layout's and `can_join` is the Graph's). There is **no COOLANT lane** in MVP: it would join the home reactor's coolant loop to B's leaking loop at the first weld, a hazard beyond "one weld" and beyond the eight faults. Layout's `fixture_airlock` and `fixture_derelict_b_socket` carry COOLANT + POWER lanes as "fixture content, not a design decision" (LY:1258); they are schema examples and are **not changed** (stated in Cross-References so no one files a conflict). The Graph's `can_join` rejects `PORT_INCOMPAT` (LS-15), so each lane's node must be real and expose the matching port type; the lane nodes sit in the dock rooms so the seam edge stays far under `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` 1152.

| Socket | Section | Facing | `line_px` | `start_px` (span) | Hatch | POWER lane node | O2 lane node |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sock_airlock_e` | `home_ship`, `airlock` | E | 720 | 672 (y 672 to 768) | `door_airlock_e` | `j_main` (CABLE_JUNCTION) | `dj_air` (DUCT_JUNCTION, bare) |
| `sock_b_w` | `derelict_b`, `b_dock` | W | 24 | 288 (y 288 to 384) | `door_b_hatch_w` | `j_b_main` (CABLE_JUNCTION) | `dj_b_dock` (DUCT_JUNCTION, bare) |

The mating predicate order is Layout's (`SOCKET_UNKNOWN`, `SOCKET_NOT_FREE`, `SAME_BODY`, `FACING_MISMATCH`, `WIDTH_MISMATCH`, `CLASS_MISMATCH`, `LANE_MISMATCH`, `GEOM_OVERLAP`); socket state runs FREE, WELDED, CAPPED or CUT_OPEN and never back. The weld offset is `offset_b = offset_a + pos_a - pos_b = (0,0) + (720,672) - (24,288) = (696, 384)`, both components multiples of 24, so B's west hull wall x [24,48) lands at [720,744) abutting the home east wall and B's hatch span y [288,384) lands on [672,768), identical to the home socket (Formula D.13).

**DA-11 Hull segments and plate positions.** Every HULL_SEGMENT has a Layout plate-position record (LY-21) and a segment `hp`; segments are 2 tiles (`HULL_SEGMENT_FACE_TILES`) with at least `SEGMENT_GAP_MIN_PX` 96 px between; every outer room has at least `HULL_RUNS_MIN` 2 disjoint exterior wall runs (`OUTER_ROOM_HULL_RUNS` is Layout's). Home 7 segments, A 4, B 5:

| Section | Segments (`room_key`: id, authored `hp`) |
|---|---|
| `home_ship` (all `hp` 10000, `HP_FULL`) | `cryo_bay`: `seg_cryo_n`, `seg_cryo_w`; `cargo_bay`: `seg_cargo_n`, `seg_cargo_e`; `airlock`: `seg_air_s1`, `seg_air_s2` (the east run is only 5 tiles and holds the 2-tile socket); `engine_room`: `seg_eng_s` |
| `derelict_a` | `a_dock`: `seg_a_dock_w` 9500; `a_life`: `seg_a_life_n` 9000; `a_machine`: `seg_a_mach_s` 7200, `seg_a_mach_e` 8000 |
| `derelict_b` | `b_breach`: `seg_b_breach` **0 (DESTROYED; the open breach, DA-28)**; `b_pump`: `seg_b_pump_n` **6999 `fatigued` (HULL-FATIGUE, DA-15)**; `b_dock`: `seg_b_dock_n` 9000; `b_reactor`: `seg_b_reactor_n` 8200; `b_spine`: `seg_b_spine_w` 7800 |

**DA-12 Hardpoints.** A hardpoint is a Layout declaration (LY-20); one is authored per module node (DA-21), plus one vacant slot on `home_ship`. A LOAD hardpoint (kind 13) carries `accepted_subtypes` because a heater and a lamp share a port signature. `compatible_defs(h)` is at least 1 for every hardpoint. Counts: `home_ship` 27 (26 module nodes, among them the dedicated beacon lamp node `bcn_main` of DA-24, + the vacant `hp_cargo_heater`, a heater-subtype slot in `cargo_bay` that exists so PWR-OVL has a reachable trigger, DA-20), `derelict_a` 18, `derelict_b` 22; world 67. `home_ship`'s 27 fits `MAX_HARDPOINTS_PER_SECTION` 32 (range 12 to 64; raised from 24 on 2026-10-27 for this GDD's DA-12 / C-4, so 5 spare), and Layout's hardpoint-count lint passes it. The closed alternative, removing three of the five home vents to reach 24 (Route 2), coupled to unsimulated gas equalisation and is not taken. Advisory `socket_tag` values on hardpoints: `lamp`, `beacon`, `heater`, `breaker`, `scrubber`, `o2_tank`, `vent`, `pump`, `radiator`, `reservoir`, `fuel_tank`, `reactor`, `battery`, `door_motor`.

#### C. The three floor plans

All coordinates are section-local px on the 24 px lattice; a wall strip is 24 px thick between rooms (LY-5). `T0` is the authored initial room temperature in mC (the Graph relaxes it toward `T_AMB_MC` -30000 while the section is loaded); values other than the cryo bay's are design targets checked by Formula D.18. Fixture columns are `fixtures_max / authored` per Layout D.16 (`min(6, 1 + floor_div(V, 8))`, `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter AC-58]`).

##### C.1 `home_ship` (HOME; `size_tiles` 16 x 18; V total 182)

The cryo bay adopts Layout's `fixture_cryo_excerpt` geometry verbatim (origin (48, 96), 7 x 6 tiles, `V` 42, `exposed_tiles` 13, door `door_cryo_s` on the south wall at start 240, hinge LOW, `area_max` 64, heater hardpoint `hp_cryo_heater`, coolant anchor `anc_cryo_coolant`, lamp anchor `fx_cryo_lamp`, `frame_exception` true), because nothing argues for a different cryo bay and it keeps every Layout acceptance true for free; it puts the far wall at 7 tiles (336 px) inside `frost_reach(-2000 mC)` of 544 px at the F&T defaults (the 336 px figure in F&T is a knob floor on large `since_steps`, never a reach).

```
y5..y9   B B B B | S    | R R R R R R R R          B = b_dock  S = b_spine (3 x 13)  R = b_reactor (8 x 5)
y10..y11         | S    |   (72 px exterior notch)
y12..y16         | S    | P P P P | V V V V V V    P = b_pump (4 x 5)   V = b_breach (6 x 5, vacuum)
                                                     tree: b_dock - b_spine - { b_reactor , b_pump - b_breach }
```

After the weld, `derelict_b` sits at offset (696, 384): `b_dock` occupies x [744,936), y [576,816), clear of the home `airlock` (x below 696) and of `spine_corridor` (x below 672, y below 552); the corridor's east hull wall x [672,696) and B's west dock wall x [720,744) are separated by the airlock's east wall [696,720), so no rect pair overlaps and `GEOM_OVERLAP` does not fire (D.13).

#### D. Time, faults and their staging

**DA-13 Derelict time behaviour: three Graph mechanisms, never "dormant until powered".** The thread's sentence ("derelict partitions are dormant until powered, then `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` 500 permille applies") maps onto three separate Graph mechanisms, none of which is "dormant until powered":

- **(a) Load-dormancy** (LS-17): a section whose derelict is not loaded (zoomed out, left behind) has its slots skipped by every phase, its state frozen, no fast-forward on return, and `can_join` returns `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`. This is driven by *load*, not by power.
- **(b) Compression** (LS-25; Graph D.9 derived line): every endogenous `hp` loss on a loaded DERELICT section runs at `rate_pm` = 10^6 / `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` = 10^6 / 500 = 2000 (the registry range of the constant is 250 to 1000 permille): wear and overload double; gas physics, heat and supply do not.
- **(c) Dead-fault wake** (LS-25): a subject at `hp` at or below `HP_FAIL` on an unserved branch emits `intensity_pm` 0 until power or coolant reaches it.

Authoring consequence: a derelict's authored `hp` values are the values the player meets on every return, because (a) freezes them; only the player's acts, the weld, or time spent *loaded* change them. The registry unit string (corrected 2026-10-27) carries no "once powered" clause, matching LS-25. The one player-facing sentence is the one in Player Fantasy.

**DA-14 Three classes of authored fault; exactly eight live instances.** The Emitter fixes "exactly 8 authored faults" (EM-24) and the Graph lists eight fixtures (LS-26), while LS-26's prose for derelict B also names a scrammed reactor, a hull breach and a coolant leak behind the overheat. The classes resolve it:

1. **LIVE faults** — exactly eight instances, one per LS-26 fixture. Each is counted for `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` and must reproduce its Graph D.15 authored values within +-15 % at its trigger configuration (LSG:1127; headless test `FAULT_D15_DRIFT`, DA-42). A live fault whose `hp` is at or below `HP_FAIL` on an unserved branch at build (#5 only: `j_a_main`, `hp` 2000) is authored with the DEAD-fault fields of DA-17 and shows a dried trail until served. Fault #6 (`pump_a`, `hp` 6000, DEGRADED, STARVE) is not FAILED, carries no `fail_since_step`, and is flow-starved rather than dried. Each is counted once, by `fault_id`.
2. **Authored STATES, not faults** — B's `r_b` REACTOR in SCRAM (cleared by V12, the hand crank, M11), B's already-open hull breach (`seg_b_breach`), A's dead bus (the broken junction is fault #5, the bus is its consequence) and the partial-charge batteries. A state emits no trail of its own beyond the Graph's consequence for it.
3. **DEAD faults** — dried trails per art bible 6.4 and 6.5: `intensity_pm` 0, `since_steps` at least `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` `[PLACEHOLDER: Failure & Trail]`, authored per IDB-24 and LS-25 (DA-17). Not among the eight, not counted for glow (intensity 0 draws no glow row) until woken, and not story slots.

**DA-15 The canonical fault table.** (registry: `mvp_fault_allocation`) One row per live instance; `T_*` clocks are loaded-section time; HOME and DERELICT clocks differ by the factor of DA-13 (b). Every authored value reproduces the Graph's D.15 fixture; the derivations are Formulas D.9, D.14, D.15, D.19 and D.20.

The authored STATES: **S-1** `r_b` SCRAMmed (cold enough that V12 is not `BLOCKED` `STILL_HOT`, `ft_b` stock above zero, `res_b` at 16,000 mL so the restart chain is feasible, D.21; witness: no node lantern since a scrammed reactor is not running, EM-14, plus the Graph's row-9 rim glow); **S-2** `seg_b_breach` `hp` 0 bounding vacuum `b_breach` (witness: the hole drawn by the Graph's hull row at size `l` and the airflow streaks, never a static tile, VA-12); **S-3** A's dead bus (witness: dark fixtures, static Graphite pipe patterns); **S-4** batteries `b_a1` `stock` 540,000 dJ, `b_a2` `stock` 1,440,000 dJ and `b_b1` `stock` 432,000 dJ (15 %, 40 % and 12 % of `BATT_CELL`; witness: the residual lamps).

Glow check (EM D.15 I-CAPS (c); Formula D.12): the welded body (home + B) can carry #2, #3, #4, #8 and B's woken SCRAM rim at once, and a woken dead coolant fault once V12 runs: at most 6 simultaneous glow rows, so `ceil(1.25 x 6)` = 8 at most and the common case 5 gives 7, both at or below `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` 10 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]`. This list is the "declared cascade hops" the Emitter asks Authoring to publish. Derelict A alone: the broken-breaker arc (1 glow row; STARVE and FREEZE have none) gives 2. The welded-body list counts B's woken coolant fault conservatively (a coolant LEAK has no glow row, EM-17), so its worst case of 6 rows is an overcount that breaches no cap.

**DA-16 Fault staging at wake.** The amended R5 (Checkpoint rulings Q1) is the rule: *exactly one fault is in view from the wake room (two under the OQ-DA-18 fallback; see the staging trigger at the end of this rule); the second home clock (FUEL-DRIP) reaches FAILED no earlier than 10 minutes after wake, its lethal transition is a recoverable SCRAM (V1 on `pf_feed`, then V12), and its tell is in view from the first room the player exits into.* At build: CLT-LEAK and FUEL-DRIP are ACTIVE; O2-SHIP is flow-latent behind a closed valve under the closed-valve staging (with the isolated clock below; it flows behind an open valve under the fallback); PWR-OVL is latent (it needs a player-installed load); #5, #6 and #7 are on derelicts and #8 needs the weld. At wake exactly one FAULT_GLOW row is active (FUEL-DRIP, row 1/FUEL); CLT-LEAK is a coolant LEAK with no glow row (EM MVP slice rows 1/FUEL, 1/GAS, 2, 3, 7, 9, EM-17) and O2-SHIP is at intensity 0 behind the closed valve (it adds a second active glow, 1/GAS, only under the ASK-G1 fallback that opens `v_o2_cryo` at wake). Routed to Attention: `N_GLOW_DARK` at MVP is 1 under the closed-valve staging and 2 under the fallback that ships until ASK-G1 is accepted (its AT-6 / F-16 text reads "the two glows authored active at wake, DA-16"). **The FUEL-DRIP witness (P2):** `door_engine_n` stands OPEN at wake, aligned with `door_cryo_s` at x 240 across the 3-tile corridor (C.1), so the Fuel Ember glow row at the `pf_feed` flange is in line of sight the moment the cryo door opens; the second tell is cross-room, reachable before FAILED, and not behind a closed door. (orchestrator ruling: the original R5 asked that no two lethal clocks run in the first ten minutes and staged FUEL-DRIP behind a closed `v_fuel_feed`. The systems-designer verified against the Graph's fuel loop that a closed feed valve is `path_cap` 0, the running home reactor starves and SCRAMs about 10 s after boot, `fuel_rem` is a sub-millilitre burn carry and not a buffer (so the level-designer's `FUEL_BUFFER_MIN` knob is invalid and is dropped), and no `hp` below `LEAK_HP` on a HOME section can give a time-to-FAILED beyond 10.5 min. Options were: accept it from wake (O1), relocate it to B (O3, which moves a second wear clock onto a wreck that already carries HULL-FATIGUE and a restart chain), arm it later (a new persisted Graph field), or author the home reactor scrammed at wake (which moves the first-ten-minutes verb from V3 to V12 and rewrites LSG:839). O1 is adopted with the witness condition above: Graph D.15 itself sizes FUEL-DRIP as a "10 min (8.5 to 11.5)" clock, the two SCRAMs are 1.8 min apart on different loops, in different rooms, with one patch each, the verb is learned on the first, and both have their endogenous tell. Checkpoint rulings Q1 accepted this.) Closing a valve upstream of a LEAK-mode subject stops its *flow* and its trail (mode LEAK stays, intensity 0) but, under the Graph's D.9 text read literally, not its `hp` wear; that reading is ASK-G1 (OQ-DA-18) and is carried into every clock above as the conservative case. **ASK-G1 is BLOCKING for the closed-valve staging of O2-SHIP** (Checkpoint rulings Q5): until the Graph owner answers, the literal reading binds content, and `d_cryo_exposed` (`hp` 2000, isolated behind the closed `v_o2_cryo`, mode LEAK, `intensity_pm` 0) erodes to DESTROYED in ceil(2000 / 16) = 125 periods = 20.8 min of HOME time from wake with no perceivable tell, and DESTROYED is `NOT_REPAIRABLE` (LS-10, M7): a G-0 and P2 breach for a player who never opens the valve (D.14). Fallback if the Graph declines to gate wear on `intensity_pm` above 0: author `v_o2_cryo` OPEN at wake (the hiss is the tell; the served `scr_main` holds the pool; the LSG D.15 O2-SHIP pool clock begins only when the scrubber loses power, which is the Graph's own "scrubber unpowered" fixture condition) and teach V5 elsewhere. **Which staging ships (the one trigger every section of this GDD uses):** the closed-valve staging ships only if the Graph owner accepts ASK-G1 before MVP content lock; if ASK-G1 is declined or still unanswered at content lock, the valve-OPEN fallback ships. Under the fallback the wake room shows two faults (the CLT-LEAK frost and the O2-SHIP 1/GAS glow and hiss at `d_cryo_exposed`, both in the cryo bay by LS-26). P2/G-0 safety outranks the one-fault-in-view rule, so the amended R5 reads: *exactly one fault is in view from the wake room (two under the OQ-DA-18 fallback, both with tells and neither lethal inside 9 minutes).* (Decision logged 2026-10-27, final advisor review.)

**DA-17 Dead faults and back-dating.** A DEAD fault (and any live fault unserved at build) is authored as the **same `def_id` as its live twin** (IDB-24: no `_dead`, `_cold` or `_dormant` def exists), at `hp` at or below `HP_FAIL` on a branch whose supply is unserved, with `fail_since_step` = -(`DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` + `DRY_MARGIN_STEPS`) = -(12000 + 2400) = **-14400** at the placeholder, so that at `step_no` 0 its `since_steps` is already above the dried threshold (Formula D.11; the field is named `fail_since_step` by Failure & Trail, FT:317). Whether M1 `instantiate_section` accepts a negative `fail_since_step` is the single ASK to the Graph owner (OQ-DA-1); until it answers, FT AC-44 is BLOCKED-ON that answer. `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` is `[PLACEHOLDER: Failure & Trail]` and is never restated here as a fact; if F&T raises it within its registered range the `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED` lint fails, which is the intended tripwire. The rule splits by subject kind: non-leaky kinds (CABLE_JUNCTION, SCRUBBER, PUMP, REACTOR, LOAD, battery) are stable at `hp` at or below `HP_FAIL` (no wear without duty), while leaky kinds (DUCT, PIPE, tanks) wear under the literal D.9 reading, so each carries its loaded-time-to-DESTROYED in the table below (Formula D.14) and `DEAD_FAULT_STILL_REPAIRABLE` (DA-42) asserts that ledger exactly: repairable before `T_destroy`, DESTROYED after it. Under the literal D.9 reading B's dead coolant leak `pb_leak` is a 15.2-minute window of B-loaded time (shorter than `T_SORTIE_LOADED_MAX` 30 min), after which the reactor-restart chain of B is unreachable (ASK-G1, DA-16).

| Section, room | Dead fault | Subject | `hp` | `forced_mode` | `fail_since_step` | Dried trail form (art bible 6.5) | Loaded time to DESTROYED (literal D.9, D.14) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A, `a_machine` | dead coolant leak | PIPE `pa_frozen` (`pj_a` to `rad_a`) | 2500 | FREEZE | -14400 | frost fan at a lagged elbow, reaching the door sill | not applicable while forced FREEZE (not in mode LEAK) `[UNV: Graph]`; if thawed, 79 periods = 13.2 min |
| A, `a_machine` | dead arc (this is live #5) | CABLE_JUNCTION `j_a_main` | 2000 | none | -14400 | radial scorch at the junction box | stable (non-leaky) |
| B, `b_pump` | dead coolant leak "behind the overheat" (the verdict's mechanical cause) | PIPE `pb_leak` | 2900 | none | -14400 | greyed frost fan with no drip | 91 periods = 15.2 min of B-loaded time |
| B, `b_breach` | dead integrity | `seg_b_breach` | 0 (DESTROYED) | none | -14400 | branching hairlines through ribs | not applicable; whether a DESTROYED subject emits a dried descriptor is Failure & Trail's (OQ-DA-11), and the answer also decides whether S-2's open hole can be drawn at all (DA-28, VA-12, `HOLE_WITHOUT_OPENING`); fallback order: `seg_b_breach` at `hp` 1500 (FAILED, BREACH mode, a descriptor exists, patchable with zero parts), then a dead non-leaky power fault on a branch V12 never serves |

(orchestrator ruling: `pb_leak` is authored at 2900, not the systems-designer's 2200, which erodes to DESTROYED in 11.5 min and would make the restart chain unreachable; 2900 is still at or below `HP_FAIL` and gives 15.2 min.) A has 2 dried trails (a cold wreck, not a breach); B has 2 and falls to 1 if F&T answers that a DESTROYED subject emits none (`TRAIL_COUNT_AREA` is WARN-level; art bible 6.4 asks 2 to 4 for a breached section). No dried fuel stain is authored on either wreck (the verdict grammar is fully served by coolant, power and hull dead faults, and F&T's own fuel stop is untested at MVP). O2 failure leaves no trail (FT-18), so a dead O2 subject is read by absence (undisturbed dust, a dark lamp-free room) and not by a trail.

**DA-18 No orphan low-`hp` subject.** Any authored `hp` strictly below `LEAK_HP` 4000 on a DUCT, PIPE, O2_TANK, RESERVOIR or FUEL_TANK node *is* a fault (the Graph gives it a leak the moment it loads) and must appear in the fault or dead-fault table; any `hp` strictly below `BREACH_HP` 4000 on a HULL_SEGMENT *is* a breach and must appear in the fault or state table. A reservoir that is merely low authors its *stock* low and its `hp` at or above `LEAK_HP`. Lint `ORPHAN_LOW_HP` (ERROR, CI).

#### E. Placement tables: nodes, power, modules, lamps, parts, loose items, the visible unknown

**DA-19 Node placement conventions and the node ledger.** Every placed node carries `node_ref` (a template-local string), `kind` (the Graph's LS-3 id), `def_id` (an Item DB string; empty for COMPARTMENT, HULL_SEGMENT and the bare junction nodes `pj_*`, `fj_*`, `dj_*`, which have no hardpoint and are not salvageable), `room_key`, and `hp` only when it differs from a fresh node's `hp_start` 10000 (`HP_FULL`; IDB). An unknown `def_id` fails the lint (`PLACEMENT_DEF_UNKNOWN`), never the runtime (IDB Interactions row). Authored `hp` overrides on derelicts are Authoring's numbers, not the def's (IDB:228). No placement kind exists outside modules, parts, consumables, fixtures, the beacon, faults, sockets, hardpoints, plate positions, spawn cells and the empty story-slot list: no entity, no weapon.

Rooms and forced modes of derelict A's coolant run (Checkpoint rulings C-F): `pa_frozen`, `pj_a`, `rad_a` and `res_a` all carry `room_key` `a_machine` (T0 -22000 mC, below `FREEZE_ON_MC` -20000), and **every coolant PIPE, RESERVOIR, RADIATOR or liquid tank in `a_machine` carries `forced_mode` FREEZE at build** (`pa_frozen`, `res_a`, `rad_a`; `pj_a` is a bare junction with no liquid of its own), otherwise it would freeze-fail at load as an unlisted endogenous transition. Whether the Graph's freeze check reads a RADIATOR is `[UNV: Graph]`; forcing it is harmless because the room's heat rejection is moot at -22000 mC. The lint `FROZEN_ROOM_UNFORCED` (DA-36, DA-40) asserts it for any room with `T0` below `FREEZE_ON_MC`.

Design edge counts (the tree rule: a loop island of n nodes needs n - 1 edges, and DA-9 forbids cycles; the Graph's own enumeration governs and may also count host edges): `home_ship` power 16 + O2 7 + coolant 5 + fuel 2 + OPENING 4 = **34**; `derelict_a` power 12 + O2 3 + coolant 3 + OPENING 3 = **21**; `derelict_b` power 13 + O2 6 + coolant 4 + fuel 2 + OPENING 4 = **29** (a hatch is a socket seam, not an OPENING, before the weld, LY-15). LS-26's "about 55 / about 45 edges" are loose estimates; Formula D.2 closes the apparent gap (a count below target is INFO, never a defect).

**DA-20 Power trees are published.** A cable's load is the sum of served power in the subtree below it (Graph D.6 derived line), so a cable's utilisation depends on its place in the tree, not on the ship's total; the tree is therefore authored data (lints `POWER_TREE_MISSING`, `OVERLOAD_WINDOW`). **Home** (17 power nodes, 16 CABLE edges): the reactor `r_main` feeds the hub junction `j_main` through the **root edge `cab_trunk`**, so every LOAD sits below it; the battery `b_main` ties to `j_main` through `cab_batt`; `j_main` feeds the cryo-branch junction `j_cryo` through `cab_cryo` and, directly, the ten loads `lamp_air_1`, `dm_air_hatch`, `lamp_eng_1`, `lamp_eng_2`, `pump_main`, `scr_main`, `lamp_cargo_1`, `lamp_corr_1`, `lamp_corr_2` and `bcn_main` (the beacon lamp, SYSTEM-switched, 0 W while the beacon is off and 20 W while it is on); `j_cryo` feeds `lamp_cryo_1`, `dm_cryo_s` and `htr_cryo`.

**Welded body (S3, S4).** The seam CABLE sub-edge between `j_main` and `j_b_main` is authored with its seam breaker OPEN at weld (Layout socket lane state; Assembly MSA). With `r_b` SCRAMmed, closing it hangs B's bus (6 lamps 120 W + `dm_b_hatch` 120 W + `scr_b` 600 W + `pump_b` 400 W = 1240 W) below `cab_trunk`: L 3120 W, supply-clamped to S 2500 W, rho 1156, 9 bp per step, DESTROYED in 1,112 steps. The design rule is therefore: the seam breaker is closed only after V12 restarts `r_b`, which then serves B's bus on its own side of the tree; closing it on a dead B is a player error whose tell is the dimming home lamps and the trunk scorch (D.9). With `r_b` running, the home tree is unchanged (standing 1880 W, 1900 W with the beacon lit) and B's 1240 W is carried by `r_b`; how the Graph apportions the two sources once both run is OQ-DA-34 `[UNV: Graph]`.

| Load (W, `req_w`) | Count | Sum | Where in the tree |
|---|---|---|---|
| lamps (`mod_lamp`, 20 W each; the beacon lamp `bcn_main` excluded) | 7 | 140 | 1 below `j_cryo` (`lamp_cryo_1`), 6 below `j_main` |
| door motors (`mod_door_motor`, 120 W) | 2 | 240 | `dm_cryo_s` below `j_cryo`; `dm_air_hatch` below `j_main` |
| heater `htr_cryo` (`mod_heater`, 500 W, tier 3) | 1 | 500 | below `j_cryo` |
| scrubber `scr_main` (600 W) | 1 | 600 | below `j_main` |
| coolant pump `pump_main` (400 W) | 1 | 400 | below `j_main` |
| **Standing load below `cab_trunk` (all lamps on, cryo branch closed, beacon off)** | | **1880 W** | `cab_trunk` `cap` 2161 W gives rho_pm 869 |
| Beacon on (`bcn_main` +20 W) | | 1900 W | rho_pm 879 |
| At wake (`j_cryo` open: minus lamp 20 + motor 120 + heater 500; beacon dark) | | 1240 W | rho_pm 573 |

The cryo branch cable `cab_cryo` has `cap` 1500 W (its subtree is 640 W; a second heater there would still sit at 76 %), and every leaf cable has `cap` at least 1.5 x its load, so no cable other than `cab_trunk` is ever a fault source. `cab_trunk` `cap` 2161 W is solved by Formula D.9 so that one added heater lands inside the Graph's PWR-OVL band both with the beacon off (trigger 2,380 W, rho_pm 1101) and on (2,400 W, rho_pm 1110); the legal `cap` range is 2159 to 2163 W. The beacon has its own node precisely so that it can be a SYSTEM-switched load without touching any fixture (DA-24), and because Attention may switch it at any moment the window must hold in both states. The reactor supply `S` is 2500 W `[PLACEHOLDER: Item DB out_w_rated]`. **Derelict A** (12 power edges): `b_a2` (the bus battery) feeds `j_a_main` (the broken breaker) which feeds `lamp_a_dock_1`, `lamp_a_spine_1`, `lamp_a_spine_2`, `lamp_a_mach_1`, `lamp_a_mach_2`, `scr_a`, `pump_a` and `htr_a`; the residual island is `b_a1` with `lamp_a_life`, tied to the bus by `j_a_res` (OPEN at build). **Derelict B** (13 power edges): `r_b` (SCRAMmed) feeds `j_b_main` (the seam lane node) which feeds six lamps, `dm_b_hatch`, `scr_b` and `pump_b`; the residual island is `b_b1` with `lamp_b_breach`, tied to the bus by `j_b_res` (OPEN at build). A cross-room cable (a trunk or a coolant run that crosses a partition) is one polyline per edge whose wall penetration Layout's lint does not check; whether that is legal is Layout's (OQ-DA-5).

**DA-21 The module faucet is the node list.** Every authored module node (a node with a `def_id` on a hardpoint) is exactly one salvage instance (IDB-29), and richness is placement density, part supply and authored `hp`, never a def stat (IDB:1135). `derelict_a` offers 18 module nodes, `derelict_b` 22, `home_ship` 26 (the home-ship's are the base, not a faucet the player should strip, but a node is a node). | A | `j_a_main` (`mod_breaker_junction`) | 2000 (FAILED) | the broken breaker, fault #5 |
| A | `pump_a` (`mod_coolant_pump`) | 6000 | the pump-stall subject, fault #6 |
| A | `pa_frozen`, `res_a` | 2500 (forced FREEZE); `res_a` at or above `LEAK_HP` with low stock | the dead coolant run (DA-17) |
| B | `scr_b` (`mod_scrubber`) | 6000 | a worn but running-grade scrubber (the weld donor's air) |
| B | `pb_leak` | 2900 | DA-17 |
| B | `seg_b_pump_n` / `seg_b_breach` | 6999 `fatigued` / 0 | DA-11, DA-15 |
| A, B, home | hull segments | DA-11 | |

**DA-22 Fixture counts: exact, per room, per legal loaded set.** (registry: `mvp_fixture_counts`) Layout D.16 bounds every room by `1 <= n <= min(6, 1 + floor_div(V, 8))` (`FIXTURE_COUNT_ROOM`) and Emitter AC-58 bounds the world at 28 fixtures per loaded set (`FIXTURE_COUNT_WORLD`), both `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter AC-58]`. A dark fixture is still a FIXTURE (EM-13: a lamp is dark when its node is dead), so every derelict room carries at least one housing; the correct sentence is "derelicts have no *powered* fixtures except the residual light", never "derelicts have no fixtures". One powered fixture is one Graph LOAD node (EM-11). Each fixture row gives `local_id` (`node_ref`), `switch_on` at build and service at build, and every emitter placement carries a non-null `priority` set by the Emitter's rule (EM AC-58; `EMITTER_PRIORITY_NULL`).

World totals are linted per legal loaded set (DA-32): `{home}` 8, `{home, A}` 14, `{home, B}` 15, `{home+B}` 15, `{home+B, A}` 21, all at or below 28. Home LOAD nodes number 11 (7 lamps, the beacon lamp `bcn_main`, two door motors, one heater) against LS-26's "about 8 LOADs"; under the one-sided tolerance of Formula D.2 that passes (over by 3, tolerance `max(floor(8 x 20 / 100), 3)` = 3; the lint warns only when over exceeds the tolerance, so 3 passes with no margin). Every room can be re-lit and un-lit (`fixtures_min` 1; the player switches a lamp with the Emitter's `set_switch`, whose PLAYER-origin form toggles against the true `switch_on`, IA Interactions row), which is what makes restraint (P3) possible.

**DA-23 The EMERGENCY lamp and the wake light.** One FIXTURE `fx_cryo_lamp` in `cryo_bay` with `node_ref` 0 (self-powered), `fixture_subkind_emergency` (`#FFCC66`), holding shadow slot 6 (`SHADOW_SLOTS_MAX` 6 `[PLACEHOLDER: Lighting]`), never modulated by Attention's tell (LO-20); it is the only `node_ref` 0 fixture in the world (`SELF_POWERED_LAMP_COUNT`, EM-11, EM-24). There is **no cryo-pod interior light in MVP**: the cryo pod carries no light; `fx_cryo_lamp` is the bay's only EMERGENCY light. Lighting Ask E-H and OQ 6 are answered "none"; ART 2.2's "pod interior light dies within the first minute" is routed to the art-director as a Vertical Slice beat, and any later pod light is a real FIXTURE placement (a subkind and a `base_pm`) whose `output_pm` falls in the simulation, never a Lighting-only glow (LO:451), and it never takes shadow slot 6 from `fx_cryo_lamp`. Lint `WAKE_SINGLE_LIGHT` (ERROR, CI): at step 0 the only lit emitter in the `cryo_bay` compartment is `fx_cryo_lamp`, and the beacon is dark. (orchestrator ruling: the art-director read ART 2.2's "darkest state in the game" as the whole ship dark at wake; R8 authors the other home fixtures `switch_on` true with only the cryo branch unserved, and a reactor running at wake serves them. The lint is scoped to the cryo-bay compartment, the room the player wakes in; Checkpoint rulings Q2 confirmed the scope: ART 2.2 binds the wake room, ART 2.4's "dark" is a different state, and lit adjoining rooms behind a closed door do not break the wake. A one-line ART 2.2/2.4 note is routed to the art-director, non-blocking.)

**DA-24 Lanterns and the beacon.** Lanterns are NODE_LANTERNs derived from a *running* node (EM-14), one per machine node of a kind EM-14 names: `home_ship` 3 (`r_main`, `pump_main`, `scr_main`), `derelict_a` 2 (`pump_a`, `scr_a`), `derelict_b` 3 (`r_b`, `pump_b`, `scr_b`); world 8 against a cap of 20, and at most `LANTERN_LIGHTS_MAX` 8 `[PLACEHOLDER: Lighting]` in view (LO-23, a subset of `NONSHADOW_LIGHTS_MAX` 24 `[PLACEHOLDER: Lighting]`). A lantern on a dead or SCRAMmed machine is dark (the failed scrubber never lights; `r_b` lights only after V12), which is also the truth witness for the pump stall. There is **exactly one BEACON** in the world data, on `home_ship` in `spine_corridor`, with line of sight to `door_cryo_s`, `gates_actions` false, authored `beacon_phase` and `beacon_dir` (the Emitter's units; the design intent is a sweep that crosses the cryo-bay door) and `node_ref` = `bcn_main`, a **dedicated LOAD node** (`mod_lamp`, 20 W) with its own hardpoint. (Closes OQ-DA-9 as "no sharing": the Emitter's state table makes `set_beacon` off issue M14 `set_load_request(node_ref, 0)`, which on a node shared with `lamp_corr_2` would zero that fixture's draw while `fx_corr_2` still shines on its tier's `served_pm` (EM-11), a lamp lit for free and a P1 lie. The beacon is SYSTEM-switched, so it also shifts PWR-OVL's trigger rho; D.9 holds the window with the beacon off and on.) `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` is 128. Attention (designed, AT-18) submits `set_beacon` (SYSTEM only) to the one beacon the data holds; the placement is this GDD's data row, and Attention neither moves nor adds a beacon. Zero beacons on A and B. `BEACON_COUNT` (ERROR, BOOT).

**DA-25 The part ledger, the spare breaker and G-0.** (registry: `mvp_part_ledger`) The Item DB's advisory band is `part_cover_pm = floor(1000 x parts_supplied / full_repairs_demanded)` within `PART_COVER_MIN_PM` 500 to `PART_COVER_MAX_PM` 800 (IDB F-11; cited, not minted here). Demand is 7 full-repairable subjects (IDB F-11): the pinhole DUCT, the O2 seam, the overloaded CABLE, the broken CABLE_JUNCTION, the coolant PIPE, the fuel PIPE and the fatigued HULL_SEGMENT (the pump stall is STARVE and takes no part; the SCRAM is cleared by a reset). Supply is **5 loose parts: A 2, B 3**, none on `home_ship`: floor(1000 x 5 / 7) = **714 permille**.

| Part | Placed on (slot) | Repairs (family) | Reached by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `part_hull_plate` (the 30 kg object, SE:73) | A, `a_spine` floor | fatigued HULL_SEGMENT `seg_b_pump_n` | first sortie; a heavy stowed part (STOWED, stack_max 1, HEAVY cue, SE-28; movement unchanged, IDB:280) |
| `part_cable_splice` | A, `a_machine` floor | overloaded trunk CABLE | first sortie, inside the lethal cold room |
| `part_pipe_fitting` x 2 | B, `b_pump` floor; B, `b_reactor` crate | coolant PIPE `p_cryo_rad`, fuel PIPE `pf_feed` | second sortie |
| `part_duct_coupling` | B, `b_breach` floor | exposed DUCT `d_cryo_exposed` | second sortie, across the vacuum |

Family cover: pipe 2 of 2, hull 1 of 1, duct 1 of 2, cable 1 of 2. Uncovered by choice (logged): the O2 seam (a SEAM edge whose full-repair capability is itself routed, IDB-20) and the broken junction (covered in kind by the spare breaker). No `part_machine_kit` is placed (a full repair of the salvaged scrubber is a Vertical Slice question, OQ-DA-12). **One spare `mod_breaker_junction`** (`condition_bp` 10000) sits on A in `a_life`: a module, not part supply (IDB OQ 19, a recommendation), and it is the fix for fault #5. **No `curio_data_core`** is placed in MVP. **G-0:** the MVP is always survivable with zero parts: every home-ship live fault is patchable to `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000 and re-patched as the patch decays (the treadmill), and A's broken junction at `hp` 2000 reaches `PATCH_CAP_BP` by patch (IDB AC-42; headless test `G0_ZERO_PART_SURVIVABLE`). Access order matters for pacing, and is deliberate scarcity (P5): the first fault of the game, the cryo-bay coolant pipe, cannot be fully repaired until the second sortie (about 45 to 55 minutes in), so a player carries about 20 patch actions on the leaky home faults before their first full repair. **Guarded by a playtest target, not retuned (CD-GDD-ALIGN concern, applied):** the MVP core hypothesis is that a 30+ minute solo session is tense and absorbing, and a treadmill of about 20 patches on a 7.5-minute leak cycle across three home faults (CLT-LEAK, O2-SHIP, FUEL-DRIP) can turn tension into upkeep. The ledger and the scarcity stay exactly as authored until the playtest; target **X-DA-9** (Game Feel; AC-DA-136) measures it, and if it fails the named levers are `PATCH_DECAY_STEPS` (a Graph value, LSG D.9, so a routed ask to the Graph owner and not a local edit) and the home leak count (3; a content edit here, with D.15 and AC-DA-22 re-run). A DESTROYED module node yields one part (IDB-29), so part instances during play may exceed 5; `PART_SUPPLY_EXCEEDS` bounds placements statically and the headless run counts instances. The player who welds B before carrying the A hull plate to `b_pump` lives with a 3.5-minute patch treadmill on `seg_b_pump_n` for the rest of the session (D.15, test `WELD_WITHOUT_PLATE_SURVIVABLE`); the GDD states the dependency plainly rather than gating it.
