Twelve glowsticks are placed in total (`GLOWSTICK_PLACED`; the carried start count is Suit's). No loose slot sits within 1 tile of a fault's maximum floor run (`LOOSE_ON_TRAIL_RUN`); a slot never sits on a door (Layout's `LOOSE_ON_DOOR`, cited). Seeded loose items per set: `{home, A}` 6, `{home, B}` 6, `{home+B, A}` 10.

**DA-27 The one visible unknown per wreck.** Each DERELICT template carries exactly one FIXTURE flagged `residual: true`: a `mod_lamp` LOAD (`req_w` 20 W) with `switch_on` true, on a **residual island** whose only source is one `mod_battery_cell` at authored partial charge, tied to the wreck's dead or scrammed bus by a junction that is OPEN at build (the island is closed: one LOAD, one source, one tie, `RESIDUAL_LEDGER_CLOSED`). The template carries the record `visible_unknown {emitter local_id, room_key, exterior_anchor_px, hue_class}`, read by Web Map & Transit for the exterior transit view, whose lit test is `residual_lamp_predicate` (Web Map & Transit WM-18: lit iff the lamp node is OK or DEGRADED and the battery stock is > 0); Authoring draws nothing and puts no marker on it.

| Wreck | Lamp (`local_id`, `node_ref`) | Host room | Battery, charge | Tie | `exterior_anchor_px` (on the hull run, design target) | `hue_class` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | `fx_a_res`, `lamp_a_life` | `a_life` | `b_a1`, `stock` 540,000 dJ (15 % of `BATT_CELL` 3,600,000 dJ) | `j_a_res` OPEN | (576, 84) on the north hull run | Tungsten (`#FFCC66`) |
| B | `fx_b_res`, `lamp_b_breach` | `b_breach` | `b_b1`, `stock` 432,000 dJ (12 % of `BATT_CELL`) | `j_b_res` OPEN | (948, 672) on the east hull run | Tungsten |

Clock (Formula D.6): a 20 W lamp draws 20 dJ per Graph step (200 dJ/s), so A's lamp burns 27,000 steps = **45 minutes of loaded time** and B's 21,600 steps = **36 minutes**. The clock counts *loaded* time only, because load-dormancy (DA-13 (a)) freezes an unloaded derelict with no fast-forward: the light cannot drain while the player is away, and a lamp found dark on a later visit is the honest reading of the player's own time aboard (the exterior view shows a built wreck's residual lamp lit iff its lamp node state is OK or DEGRADED and its persisted battery stock is > 0 (`residual_lamp_predicate`, Web Map and Transit WM-18), and a never-built wreck lit if `charge` > 0). (orchestrator ruling: R7's worked example of 25 % and 75 min outlives two 30-minute sessions against its own "not two sessions", and the load-dormancy rule it cites makes the example moot; 15 % and 12 % on loaded time are adopted on the level-designer's, systems-designer's and creative-director's agreement, conditional on the DRAINED lamp reading as honest and on Attention not modulating a residual lamp.) The lamp is steady and unmodulated (the gutter is the cryo lamp's grammar; the exterior unknown must be a fixed honest point). Any withdrawal from the island battery (a Suit cell top-up) shortens the lamp step for step. A closed tie on a welded or powered bus lets the bus recharge the battery (a V3 decision). B's SCRAM rim glow is an additional glow and not the residual light. Host-room rules (VA-6, lints `VISIBLE_UNKNOWN_NO_PORT`, `VISIBLE_UNKNOWN_COMPETITOR`): the host room's hull-run wall carries a `wall_port` piece within 48 px of `exterior_anchor_px` (a port rect that is SOLID but not `OCCLUDES_LIGHT`, a Layout ask, OQ-DA-19), and the host room has no other lit FIXTURE, NODE_LANTERN or glow-row fault. Lint `RESIDUAL_LIGHT_COUNT` (ERROR, BOOT): exactly one per DERELICT template, zero per HOME, on a battery-served island with `charge` > 0. Rejected: FAULT_GLOW as the unknown (its lifetime is `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`'s, not the author's); a second self-powered subkind (breaks EM-11 and AC-58 and P1). The note owed to the Item DB and Graph owners: B carries one `mod_battery_cell` although IDB:237 contrasts "derelict B a live REACTOR"; no value changes.

#### F. Hazard rooms, fairness, budgets and truth

**DA-28 Hazard rooms: the one lethal cold room, the vacuum traverse, B's breach.**

- *Lethal cold (derelict A only).* `a_machine` is authored at `T0` -22000 mC with `forced_mode` FREEZE on its liquid pipes (LSG:764: a derelict "already frozen" is a content fact, not an endogenous transition). The shifted cold onset is -20000 mC (`COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` -15000 less `INSUL_MC` 5000); the room's equilibrium sits at the plate-term stall, `ceil(100000 / (K_HULL x exposed_tiles))` = ceil(100000 / 325) = 308 mC above `T_AMB_MC` -30000 (about -29692 mC; the Graph's 182 mC is its 22-tile example, not this room), far below the onset, which satisfies Suit's export (SE-21: a lethal cold room must have equilibrium below the shifted onset) `[derived; Graph owner to confirm with LSG D.7, OQ-DA-4]`. Suit's export is why the rule exists: with `INSUL_MC` 5000 an unpowered home-ship needs about 40 minutes to reach the shifted onset (SE-21), so a cold-hazard derelict room must be authored with an equilibrium temperature below the shifted cold onset or it is no hazard. It is the only lethal-cold room in MVP (B's hazard is heat and vacuum; B's heat room is `b_reactor`, next bullet). It is entered from `a_spine` (`T0` -12000 mC) through `door_a_spine_machine`, with a dried frost fan creeping out over the door sill, visible in the beam: the tell precedes the hazard (P2). Crossing is safe and lingering is lethal: at `T0` the room reaches CRITICAL cold in about 36 s and FAILED in about 109 s (Suit's D.4 with the Suit knobs; Formula D.10), so a seven-tile crossing takes 5.6 s carrying while the patch channel inside is the lethal act. The room's heater `htr_a` and the bus battery `b_a2` sit on the branch that the spare breaker wakes, so **feeding the wreck is also how a player survives staying**; the quiet alternative (cut `scr_a` in `a_life` and leave) never enters the room. `COLD_ROOM_UNDECLARED` (ERROR): a room whose `T0` is below the shifted onset must be declared lethal, and exactly one per MVP body may be. The "exactly one lethal room" invariant is a *build-state* invariant: opening the door between `a_spine` and `a_machine` mixes them by conduction, so the lint also prints the open-door projection.
- *Heat (derelict B, `b_reactor`, after V12; `[derived; Graph owner to confirm Q_src_room]`).* `b_reactor` is authored at `T0` 42000 mC (the HOT band, 40000 and up, LSG:96), 3000 mC under `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` 45000 (registry). Suit's heat axis has no insulation shift (`e_heat` = `T` - `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC`, SE D.4), so the room is harmless as authored and its HOT shimmer is the standing tell. A SCRAMmed `r_b` has `Q_src` 0 and `T_mach` = `T_room` (LSG D.8), which is why V12 is not `BLOCKED` `STILL_HOT` (DA-35). **After V12** the restarted reactor leaks the uncaptured part of its heat into the room (`Q_src_room`; 516 dJ per step is the Graph's D.8 worked example, `[UNV: Graph]`) against a hull loss of `K_HULL` x 21 x (`T` - `T_AMB_MC`) / 100000 = 378 dJ per step at 42000 mC: the room reaches 45000 mC after about 2,770 steps (4.6 minutes, `tau_steps` 22,857) and settles near 68,300 mC (D.18). From the fifth minute lingering is damaging: at that equilibrium `e` is 23,286, `gain` is 40, `runs_critical` is 100 (10.0 s) and `runs_failed` 300 (30.0 s) by D.10 with Suit's heat gain; the 8-tile crossing is 192 ticks (3.2 s) walking and 384 ticks (6.4 s) carrying. The V12 channel completes before the room warms; cutting `scr_b` after V12 is the lingering act, and the player is told by the shimmer, never by a marker. **The tell must escalate before the damage (P2; CD-GDD-ALIGN concern, applied).** A standing shimmer from `T0` 42000 that looks the same when the room crosses `HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` would be a tell that never changes, so the presentation owners are asked for one visible *step* in the room's heat presentation (a band change in shimmer amplitude or rate, never a hue step into flesh or violet) at `HEAT_TELL_STEP_MC` (Tuning Knobs; `[PLACEHOLDER: F&T / Lighting]`, 44,700 mC as authored), which the D.18 projection reaches **2,477 steps (4.1 minutes) after V12 and 293 Graph steps (29 s, 1,758 ticks) before `t_onset_steps` 2,770**, never fewer than `W_MIN_WARNING_S` x 10 Graph steps per second = 100 steps (the 44,897 mC ceiling; AC-DA-133). F&T's shimmer today is a sev-2 stepped strip at the fitting (FT:942, FT:1087) with no onset step, which is why the ask exists (routed ask ASK-F&T-H1, Cross-References, OQ-DA-37). If the step is not provided, the AC stays BLOCKED and `b_reactor` is not releasable as a damaging room. `HEAT_ROOM_UNDECLARED` (ERROR, CI) enforces the declaration; `B_REACTOR_T_MC` and `HEAT_TELL_STEP_MC` (Tuning Knobs) are the levers.
- *Vacuum traverse.* Suit's bound (SE D.11, AC-SE-75; `RESERVE_S` 90 s `[PLACEHOLDER: Suit]`) is one-way: `traverse_s = tiles x TILE_PX / (V x TICK_RATE) <= RESERVE_S / 2`, which is at most 112 tiles at `V_WALK` 2 and 56 tiles carrying at `V_CARRY` 1: every authored vacuum traverse is walkable within `RESERVE_S / 2` (at most 112 tiles walking) or carryable at carry speed within it (at most 56 tiles carrying), and `RESERVE_WARN_S` 30 `[PLACEHOLDER: Suit]` <= `RESERVE_S / 2` (Suit A5; 30 <= 45 at the defaults). Authoring applies the carry bound to the **round trip** (R17: "and back"), the stricter reading, so a module can be carried out on the same reserve: `2 x tiles_one_way <= 56`. B's `b_breach` is about 11 tiles each way (Manhattan, 6 x 5 room), a 22-tile round trip, and when `b_pump` is itself decompressed its crossing adds 9 tiles each way: a worst case of 40 tiles against 56. `TRAVERSE_TOO_LONG` (ERROR, CI) fires on the stricter figure and reports Suit's one-way figure beside it; this closes AC-SE-75's "NOT ASSESSED" once the lint runs.
- *B's open breach.* `b_breach` is a vacuum compartment bounded by `seg_b_breach` at `hp` 0 (a DESTROYED Graph state: the module's own destroyed state, since "breached geometry is not the breach state", art bible 6.4); it is geometry authored as its original type with props displaced (on the 24 px lattice, no off-grid record, OQ-V8), at most 30 % breached kit pieces and 1 to 2 web entries from the hull side if web intrusion is in MVP scope (art-director OQ-V5). The hole is drawn by the Graph's hull-segment row at size `l`, never by a static through-hole tile (`HOLE_WITHOUT_OPENING`, VA-12). Coupling (Checkpoint rulings Q9): that row is a descriptor of the Graph subject, so if Failure & Trail answers that a DESTROYED subject emits none (OQ-DA-11), the open hole itself cannot be drawn (a P1 lie by omission), not only the dried-trail count thins; fallback order: `seg_b_breach` at `hp` 1500 (FAILED, BREACH mode, patchable with zero parts, B's traverse kept because the room is authored at vacuum), then a dead non-leaky power fault on a branch V12 never serves. The room is entered only through `door_b_pump_breach`, which the player opens; the draught across the door is the tell.

**DA-29 Escape routes, fairness and death.** Every anchor that can carry an endogenously failing descriptor has a closable door within `ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX` of route length (`ESCAPE_ROUTE_OVER_BUDGET`, ERROR). The budget is Layout's LY D.19 (not a registry entry): `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10 x `TICK_RATE` 60 x `V_RUN` 3 = **1,800 px**, which is 1,200 px at `V_WALK` 2 and 600 px carrying at `V_CARRY` 1 (Formula D.5). Layout supplies the read `route_length_px`; the lint is Authoring's. Estimates (Layout's read governs): `p_cryo_rad` to `door_cryo_s` about 336 px, `pf_feed` to `door_engine_n` about 480 px, `seg_b_pump_n` to `door_b_spine_pump` about 384 px, the longest at 27 % of budget. `ESCAPE_ROUTE_CARRY` (WARN) lists any room where a salvageable module node (a module in hand moves at `V_CARRY` 1, PC-8) shares a room with a live endogenous fault; a stowed part, the 30 kg plate included, does not change speed (IDB:280, SE-28). `CORRIDOR_HEAD_NARROW` (144 px head clearance at any anchor that can reach severity 4) is Layout's id, cited. **No authored instakill without a tell:** B's breach is entered by the player's own act through a door whose draught is the tell; A's lethal cold room is reached past a frost line the beam shows; every live fault has its endogenous 10 s fairness window (`W_MIN_WARNING_S`, LS-13, endogenous transitions only; exogenous causes are not gated, so each authored exogenous hazard needs a visible precursor and a survivable retreat). Death is lethal including friendly fire (user decision) and MVP death is restart from the last autosave, which Save/Load owns (SL; cited, not restated); **no floor plan contains a one-way point**: `RETURN_PATH_TRAVERSE` (ERROR) requires a door path from every room back to `entry_room_key`, with any vacuum or cold stretch inside the traverse bound. A sane player can always back away; a carrier (`V_CARRY`) only after dropping cargo (Horror Entities C-HE-8). No room with a single exit has a floor span under 144 px (`DEAD_END_NARROW`, ERROR, Horror Entities E-HE-26; the `pod` stub is exempt, its section always dormant); every MVP room is at least 3 tiles on its short axis, so the lint passes as authored.

**DA-30 Story slots: reserved, empty, three roles.** Every template carries `story_slots: []` (Layout's `STORY_SLOT_LIST` ERROR if absent; `MAX_STORY_SLOTS_PER_SECTION` is 0 in MVP, LY:1040). The three Vertical Slice roles CAUSE, VERB and ABSENCE and the eye-line rule (trail, then source, then hatch; art bible 6.5) are documented as that slice's grammar, and **zero slots are authored**. What *is* MVP content, because it is a Graph fact or a Layout door state and not a narrative placement, is: dried trails, door leaf states (closed and open only, DA-8) and the cryo-bay pod states (closed-frosted, open-dark, smashed). Who was in the other pods is the narrative-director's; the default is one open-dark pod (the player's) and the rest closed-frosted with no silhouette (OQ-DA-8). In MVP, no text, no names, no bodies; empty suits in three poses are Vertical Slice. This is a scope limit, not a schema closure: the slot list and its three roles are what procgen inherits, and diegetic lore fragments (Alpha; found and assembled, never a linear authored story) are the narrative-director's to define and would fill `story_slots` only in templates released to the stage that owns them. `STORY_SLOT_FILLED_IN_MVP` (ERROR, CI) accepts no waiver.

**DA-31 Containment colours and the PD-1 exceptions.** Lighting's containment lint (LO-33, PD-1) colours each body's compartment adjacency so that no two compartments within `R_DRAW_MAX_PX` 122 + `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` 24 = 146 px share a colour, and its C3 lint (rewritten 2026-10-27, LO-33) defines spill per wall face, s = max(0, r_draw - d_face - WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH), on faces backing onto another compartment (hull walls exempt), and passes a placement whose origin is at least r_draw - 24 = 98 px from each such face. Compartment sizes follow the art bible's density table and Layout, never the C3 lint (art bible 6.4 is locked; C3 is a spike input). A non-shadow FIXTURE sits at least 98 px from any interior face that backs onto another compartment where the room allows it; elsewhere it is still placed (`fixtures_min` 1) and logged as a PD-1 exception with its spill depth. A room cannot pass with any placement only when two opposing interior faces are under 2 x 98 = 196 px apart; every other MVP room has at most one interior face per axis. Recount (Authoring's estimate; the lint's output governs): **2 of 14 are PD-1 exceptions as built**, `spine_corridor` (144 px between its north and south interior faces, s 26 px at the centre line) and `b_pump` (192 px between its spine and breach faces, s 2 px at the centre line); `a_spine` and `b_spine` (144 px wide) pass only with each fixture in a stretch where one side is hull (`a_spine` clear of the `a_dock` run y [216,456); `b_spine` beside the exterior notch, y [432,552), or along `b_pump`'s run where its west side is hull), which is a placement constraint, not an exception. Whether `b_dock`'s west wall counts as backing onto another compartment after the weld (it faces the home `airlock`'s east wall across two wall strips, not lit floor) is `[UNV: Lighting]`; if it does, `b_dock` (192 px) becomes a third, post-weld-only exception. The greedy colouring of each legal body (home, A, B, and home + B after the weld) needs **3 colours**, within `CONTAIN_COLOURS` 4 `[PLACEHOLDER: Lighting]` (Formula D.8); the report is an output, run with every door closed and with every door open (the proximity edge is geometric, so both give 3), never a re-shaping instruction. If the number ever exceeds `CONTAIN_COLOURS`, that is a CONFLICT paragraph to Lighting, not a silent edit of a room.

**DA-32 Loaded sets and subject budgets.** (registry: `legal_loaded_sets`) World-total lints run per *reachable loaded set*, not over the triple (Layout D.16's wording is "over the loaded set"; the Interaction subject cap and the Emitter caps are per loaded set). The sets follow the weld graph: a set is the home body plus at most one unwelded derelict being visited, where the home body is `{home}` before the weld and `{home, B}` after it. (provisional — Web Map & Transit undesigned; orchestrator ruling: R9's three sets are replaced by the systems-designer's five, because B stays loaded as part of the home body after the weld, so a post-weld sortie to A is reachable.)

| Set | Meaning | Tiles (`TILES_WORLD_MVP` 600, WARN at 540) | Fixtures (cap 28) | Lanterns (cap 20) | Interaction subjects `S_int` = F + L_seed + C (cap 120 `[PLACEHOLDER: Interaction]`) | `S_total` = S_int + 30 (cap 192 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S0 `{home}` | the wake | 182 | 8 | 3 | 48 + 2 + 18 = 68 | 98 |
| S1 `{home, A}` | sortie to A | 300 | 14 | 5 | 76 + 6 + 18 = 100 | 130 |
| S2 `{home, B}` | sortie to B (unwelded) | 331 | 15 | 6 | 88 + 6 + 18 = 112 | 142 |
| S3 `{home+B}` | welded, no sortie | 331 | 15 | 6 | 88 + 6 + 18 = 112 | 142 |
| **S4 `{home+B, A}`** | post-weld sortie to A | 449 | 21 | 8 | 116 + 10 + 18 = **144** | 174 |

`F` is the declared interactables (hardpoints, two face subjects per door, valves, hull segments, sockets): home 48, A 28, B 40; `C` is the cap-stub worst case (at most 6 modules x 3 ports = 18); the constant 30 is `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24 + actors 4 + horrors 2 (`HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` `[PLACEHOLDER: Horror]`; registry `subject_budget`). **S4 breaches `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 by 24** while staying inside the Emitter's 192, and S2/S3 have 8 subjects of slack. Options, in order (Checkpoint rulings Q4): (1) **ASK-I1 (primary)**: ask Interaction to register interactable subjects by body adjacency or proximity and not by "loaded", so a welded-in far-away section does not spend the budget (fixes the whole class); (2) a **joint ask to Interaction and the Emitter**: `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` at least 144 *together with* `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` at least 224 (Emitter-owned, registry range 128 to 384), because Interaction's I-7 requires `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` + `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` <= 144 (75 % of `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` 192) and ceil((144 + 24) x 4 / 3) = 224; this is a two-owner change, not an Interaction knob turn; (3) **Authoring's own fallback, changing no other owner's number** (it needs only the Graph's confirmation that a VENT or LOAD may be authored bare, which DA-19 already assumes for junctions, OQ-DA-23): a named diet of at least 24 subjects in S4 (candidates and their readability cost are listed in Edge Cases); (4) gate the pod so A cannot be visited after the weld, rejected as a gate on sortie freedom. Lint `SUBJECT_BUDGET_SET` is ERROR on S0 to S3 and WARN with the shortfall on S4 until Interaction confirms its placeholder (OQ-DA-20). The runtime contract Authoring states and cannot enforce: **a dormant section must have unregistered its subjects (IA-37) or the weld breaches the subject cap**; when a registration fails the object "fails dark" and reads as a hallucination, which is a P1 violation on the content side. Authored emitters are linted per set against `MAX_EMITTERS` 96 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` and the Emitter's derived 70.

**DA-33 Prop density and the generator rules as content lints.** The art bible's generator rules 1 to 8 are CONTENT lints here, so a later generator can reuse them: (1) continuity, every authored pipe ends in a terminal fitting or continues (`PIPE_DEAD_END`); (2) one trail per source anchor, every trail maps to a Graph fault (`TRAIL_NO_SOURCE`, `TRAIL_COUNT_AREA`); (3) hero cap, at most one periodic-motion medium in a non-machine room (`HERO_CAP`); (4) one residual light per wreck (`RESIDUAL_LIGHT_COUNT`, DA-27); (5) seams only at joins (`SEAM_NOT_AT_JOIN`); (6) intrusion only in breached rooms, from the hull side (`INTRUSION_OUTSIDE_BREACH`); (7) the pipe band, rows 29 to 58 of the 72 px face (0.4 to 0.8), clear of props (`PIPE_BAND_BLOCKED`); (8) a clear path of at least one tile (`CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED`, Layout's id, cited). Densities follow art bible 6.4: cryo bay wall props 1 to 2 per 10 tiles, floor at most 10 %, 1 moving medium, 1 trail; corridor at most 1 wall prop per 10, floor at most 5 %, 0 media; machine room 3 to 4 wall props per 10, floor 20 to 30 %, 3 to 6 media; a breached derelict section as its original type but displaced, 2 to 4 dried trails, at most 30 % breached pieces; grafted home-ship rooms +10 % crew clutter. Counting conventions (so the lint is unambiguous): density is evaluated at the template's *build state* (a fault that starts later is a runtime consequence, legal); a moving medium is a medium with *periodic* motion (a drip, a rotor, a bellows, a sight-glass pulse), so the heater, drawn as a steady coil-glow ramp, is not one and the cryo bay has exactly one (the drip); a trail is one *source anchor*, however many sprites (fan, crust, floor run) F&T draws from it; wall props are counted per 10 tiles of front-face wall run (which runs bear a face is the Pipeline's, OQ-V7); floor coverage is the non-walkable set-dressing footprint over the room's floor tiles. Wall-mounted fixture housings occupy face rows 59 to 72, above the pipe band (art-director proposal for the art spike). No floor prop's lit albedo exceeds Graphite lit where a trail could run (checklist 13; `FLOOR_PROP_TOO_BRIGHT`, WARN, asset-level).

**DA-34 The truth-witness rule and the falsifier map.** Every authored subject that the Perception Filter could misrepresent (a gauge, a breaker, a valve, a salvageable module, a live fault) has at least one consequence-channel witness in the same compartment: a trail (live or dried), a frost line, a lantern on or off, a lit or dark fixture on its branch, or the room's own temperature or pressure effect. The fault table's last column is the falsifier map; AC-V5 requires a witness for every fault and state. Lint `TRUTH_WITNESS_MISSING` is **WARN in MVP and becomes ERROR when the Perception Filter lands** (so the BOOT subset does not grow). One flagged gap: A's failed scrubber is a module whose failure leaves no pixel (O2 leaves no trail, FT-18), so its witnesses are the closed-list witnesses of LSG:44 (a lamp-free dead room) plus SCAN, the honest MVP instrument (Boarding & Salvage BS-17, which answers OQ-V10); undisturbed dust and Graphite-static pipes on its branch are supporting verdict grammar, not witnesses of record. The classification of structure and props as "truth" is `(provisional — Perception Filter undesigned)`. **Instrument placements (In-World Instruments IW-31).** Gauges are In-World Instruments' surfaces; Authoring authors their placement as a Kit placement record `{instrument_id, node_ref or room_key plus quantity, mount (WALL_FACE or MACHINE_PANEL), host_face}` (the record kind is a Layout schema ask, LY-4) and lints it with `INSTRUMENT_NO_BINDING` (every Graph gauge is bound and registered as an OBJECT subject for `lit(ref)`, so none silently drops out of the lie set, Sanity EC-SN-57), `PATCH_OVER_OVERRIDE` (IW E-IW-38) and the advisory `LIE_SURFACE_FLOOR` (at least 3 lie-eligible surfaces per site, so a scan-everything habit is not dominant; Sanity D.12, AC-SN-134, OQ-SN-11). The instrument rows themselves, and a second battery reading on A as the partner for `inst_b_a1_stock`, wait on the subject budget (OQ-DA-38), because every instrument is an Interaction subject counted in D.17.

**DA-35 Authored initial state is data, listed and diffed.** Every authored initial value (`hp`, `T0`, stock, charge, `breaker_closed`, `switch_on`, valve `open`, `fail_since_step`) is a single row in the template and is read back by the headless harness as a state diff against `default_state()`, so a template edit changes `state_hash` (LY-26) and nothing is hidden. Values beyond defaults:

| Section | Authored initial state |
|---|---|
| `home_ship` | `j_cryo` `breaker_closed` false; `v_o2_cryo` VALVE `open` false if ASK-G1 is accepted before MVP content lock, else `open` true (the valve-OPEN fallback that ships while ASK-G1 is declined or unanswered, DA-16); `v_fuel_feed` and `v_clt_isolate` `open` true; `r_main` running with `ft_main` `stock_ml` full; `ot_main` `stock` 3,000,000 mGU; `res_main` `stock_ml` 20,000; `b_main` `stock` 2,160,000 dJ (60 % of `BATT_CELL`, a design target); `cab_trunk` `cap` 2161 W; `bcn_main` dark (beacon off, 0 W requested); `door_engine_n` OPEN, every other door CLOSED; `p_cryo_rad` 3800 with `fail_since_step` -60 (DA-15 #1); `d_cryo_exposed` 2000; `pf_feed` 3999; room `T` per C.1; the six lamps of the four non-cryo rooms `switch_on` true and served, `fx_cryo_2` `switch_on` true and unserved |
| `derelict_a` | `j_a_main` `hp` 2000, `breaker_closed` false; `j_a_res` `breaker_closed` false; `b_a1` `stock` 540,000 dJ, `b_a2` `stock` 1,440,000 dJ; `scr_a` 2000; `pump_a` 6000; `pa_frozen` 2500 `forced_mode` FREEZE, `fail_since_step` -14400; `res_a` and `rad_a` `forced_mode` FREEZE; `ot_a` `stock` and `res_a` `stock_ml` low (about 10 % of the def capacity, a design target); `door_a_spine_life` OPEN, other doors CLOSED; `T` per C.2 |
| `derelict_b` | `r_b` SCRAMmed, `T_mach` below `OVERHEAT_OFF_MC`, `ft_b` `stock_ml` half full; `res_b` `stock_ml` 16,000; `j_b_res` `breaker_closed` false; `b_b1` `stock` 432,000 dJ; `ot_b` `stock` half; `scr_b` 6000; `pb_leak` 2900, `fail_since_step` -14400; `seg_b_pump_n` 6999 `fatigued`; `seg_b_breach` 0; all doors CLOSED; `T` per C.3 |

**DA-36 Count tolerance is one-sided.** LS-26's "about" counts (nodes about 40 / 30 / 35; edges about 55 / 45; LOADs about 8) are targets, not bounds. `NODE_COUNT_DRIFT` (WARN) fires only when `authored > target + max(floor(target x NODE_DRIFT_PCT / 100), NODE_DRIFT_ABS_MIN)` with `NODE_DRIFT_PCT` 20 and `NODE_DRIFT_ABS_MIN` 3; a count below target is INFO, because the Graph's cost model (LSG D.16) is monotone in count, so fewer is never a defect (Formula D.2). The hard bounds are the Graph's: total authored nodes at most 120 (LSG:787; 104 here), `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 / edges 8192 (registry, irrelevant at this scale), and the 800-node content WARN (LSG:915; irrelevant at MVP scale, said so). Also lints from the Graph's content-lint criterion: the D.12 fragility sum per compartment (`FRAGILITY_SUM`, WARN above 500,000 ppm), `delta_T_eq` per heated compartment (`HEATER_DELTA_T`, WARN above 60 C; the home cryo bay's single heater is the case checked), and any liquid pipe authored below `FREEZE_ON_MC` without `forced_mode` FREEZE (`FROZEN_ROOM_UNFORCED`, ERROR, CI: any PIPE, RESERVOIR, RADIATOR or liquid tank in a room whose `T0` is below `FREEZE_ON_MC`, DA-40).

**DA-37 Nothing authored is a marker, a lie or a combat placement.** In MVP, no label, icon, arrow, rating or text on any authored piece (VA-13; VA-13's ban targets UI-like markers, not future diegetic lore fragments, which are the narrative-director's to define and would enter through `story_slots`, never as a marker); no "phantom", "false door", "fake trail" or "decoy" record in any template (the Perception Filter corrupts the module-body channel at runtime, never the data); no entity, weapon or enemy placement (tools repel); no loot roll, rarity or tier; no meter, minimap or HUD icon for the visible unknown; no flesh (hue 5 to 30, S 20 to 60 %, V 45 to 85 %) or violet (hue 270 to 330, S above 25 %) pixel in any kit sheet or item sprite, and the lit sample of Hull Grey under every Tungsten source stays at hue 36 degrees or more (`PALETTE_SANITY_BAND`, asset-level, ERROR). A wreck is monochrome before power returns: the chromatic content of a derelict frame is closed to the player's beam, the residual lamp, thrown Chemlight pools and live fault glows (AC-V2 is the measurable form of "darkness is honest"). The anti-pillar guard rails of Player Fantasy are enforced by these lints and by the review checks in Acceptance Criteria.

#### G. The kit, the lint and the workflow

**DA-38 The kit manifest is data, shaped for procgen.** The manifest (registry: `kit_manifest_schema`) is a table of rows `{piece_id, category, footprint_cell, layer, flags, variant_tokens, layout_record_kind, count_bucket}`; no row names a template, so the Vertical Slice generator (VS-A) assembles from the same rows. Categories: floor, wall, door, pipe mount, fixture housing, machinery node, prop, storytelling (empty at MVP), breached variant, shared. Cells follow art bible 8.2 (floor 48 x 48; wall 48 x 96 = face 72 + cap 24; door frame plus leaf 96 x 96; machinery node 96 x 96 with a moving-medium region of at least 16 x 16; props 48 x 48, 48 x 96, 96 x 48; seam bead 4 px Hull Grey). Layers are floor, wall face, cap and prop; flags are `SOLID`, `OCCLUDES_LIGHT` and pipe-band clearance; variant tokens are `_worn`, `_breached`, `_frosted`, `_open`, `_smashed` and the door states. This GDD does not restate LY-4's field tables: it names the record kind a piece instantiates.

| Group | Piece stems (art bible 8.5 naming) | Count (maintained + `_worn`) | Cell, layer | `layout_record_kind` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Floor | `floor_base`, `floor_plated`, `floor_threshold` (door sill) | 3 + 3 | 48 x 48, floor | Room tile / Kit placement |
| Wall | `wall_straight`, `wall_inner`, `wall_outer`, `wall_cap`, `wall_end` | 5 + 5 | 48 x 96, wall face + cap; `SOLID`, `OCCLUDES_LIGHT`; face at most 72 | Wall run |
| Wall port (exterior) | `wall_port` (round Foundry port; glass is Void Steel with one highlight) | 1 + 1 | 48 x 96; `SOLID`, **not** `OCCLUDES_LIGHT` (a Layout ask) | Kit placement on a hull run |
| Door | `door_round` frame; leaf states `closed`, `open`, `cut`, `welded` | 5 + 3 | 96 x 96 | Door (`cut`, `welded` kept for the socket seam and VS) |
| Pipe mount | `mount_bracket_straight`, `mount_bracket_elbow` | 2 + 2 | wall band 0.4 to 0.8 of the face | Anchor |
| Fixture housing | `fixture_housing`, `fixture_housing_emergency` (hanging low), `env_shared_beacon_housing` (Attention's A6 stem; a shared piece, tallied in this row at MVP) | 3 | 48 x 48 / 24 x 24; the lit state is the same sprite with the light on | Fixture anchor |
| Machinery nodes | O2, heat and coolant, power, hull-plate module | 4 | 96 x 96, medium at least 16 x 16 | Hardpoint |
| Module bodies beyond the nodes | up to 9, in four loop families sharing one silhouette grammar (O2 ring clamps, power square boxes, coolant padded collars, fuel bolted flange) | up to 9 | per Item DB `cell` | Hardpoint |
| Props | crate, locker, bunk, table, console, chair, tool rack, canister, cover plate | 9 (+ the worn set is derived) | 48 x 48, 48 x 96, 96 x 48 | Kit placement / Loose slot |
| Cryo pod wall prop | `prop_foundry_cryo_pod_closed_frosted`, `_open_dark`, `_smashed` | 3 | 48 x 96, wall | Kit placement (gap K-1) |
| Pod-dock fitting | `env_foundry_fitting_dock` | 1 | 48 x 96 on a hull run | Kit placement (gap K-2) |
| Docked podcraft placeholder | `prop_shared_podcraft_docked` | 1 | 96 x 96 prop cell (art bible 8.2 has 96 x 96 only for machinery and doors) | Kit placement (gap K-3) |
| Breached variants | torn wall straight, buckled wall corner (no through-hole) | 2 | per slice | Kit placement |
| **Dialect subtotal (Foundry)** | | **48 maintained + 14 worn = 62** | | art bible 6.3 sizes about 50 |

**About 125 pieces against the art bible's MVP sizing of about 110: about +14 %**, essentially all due to the `_worn` set and the extra module bodies. Mitigations in order: derive the `_worn` sheets from the maintained sheets by a tool pass (ADR candidate: worn-atlas derivation tool); share silhouettes per loop family; cut optional props to 8; accept the remainder and log it to the producer (AC-V12). **Kit gaps filed against the art bible:** K-1 the cryo-pod wall prop in three states is absent from ART 6.3 although 6.4 says "the pods are the wall" (and `smashed` is not an 8.5 state token); K-2 the pod-dock fitting; K-3 the docked-podcraft placeholder needs a 96 x 96 prop cell; K-4 the cap plate, plate position and ragged cut edge are absent from the shared sets though the seam bead is present; K-5 the exterior wall port; K-6 the EMERGENCY and beacon housings; K-7 the `worn` variant token; K-8 the MVP machinery-node note omits fuel and covers 3 of 13 module defs; K-9 stub pieces for severed pipe and cable ends after a cut (Boarding & Salvage and the Item DB own them). The inventory test (Layout AC-143) is that every manifest piece maps to a Layout record kind and every Layout kit-bearing record kind has at least one manifest piece; the pass line is zero unmapped in both directions (`KIT_PIECE_UNMAPPED`); a piece with no home in the schema is a Layout defect to file.

**DA-39 Visual rules adopted as content rules (art-director VA set).** VA-1 every authored piece is a Foundry kit piece named in the manifest, no off-manifest sprite. VA-2 `home_ship` uses the maintained atlas, wrecks `_worn`, no Layout wear field. VA-3 no violet or flesh pixel and lit-sample hue at least 36 degrees under every Tungsten source. VA-4 every live-fault anchor has a clear floor run at least one tile wide and a free approach, so the trail can be seen to worsen from across the room (`ROOM_EXCEEDS_FRAME` exceptions logged). VA-5 no loose slot, prop or fixture sits on a fault's maximum floor run. VA-6 the visible-unknown host room has an exterior port within 48 px of `exterior_anchor_px` and no competing lit light. VA-7 at wake the EMERGENCY lamp is the only lit emitter in the cryo bay and the beacon is dark. VA-8 every dead fault is a Graph subject; no static loop-vocabulary scorch, soot, frost, stain or puddle exists in any kit tile; **exemption:** tool scorch on a cut door leaf or ragged-cut hull piece (the crew's cutter, hue-less Void Steel, never Power Amber rimmed) is a kit state, not a loop fault. VA-9 "moving medium" means periodic motion (the heater is a steady coil glow). VA-10 every wall-flush piece has face height at most 72 px and nothing overlaps face rows 29 to 58. VA-11 web pieces appear only in a room bounded by a breached segment, entering from the hull side, drawn only under direct light. VA-12 an open breach is drawn by the Graph's hull-segment state plus the plate-position piece; static torn or buckled variants never depict a through-hole and total at most 30 % of pieces. VA-13 no label, icon, arrow or text on any authored piece; no body, no skin. VA-14 a dead room's absence is undisturbed dust (floor dust unmarked). VA-15 dust motes drift only where the compartment has air, non-directional, 1 px, slow, never imitating an airflow streak. VA-16 a dead branch's pipes are Graphite with a static pattern and a live branch carries its hue and a moving pattern, the same sheet serving both. VA-17 every authored false-able system has a world-visible truth within one beam sweep. The pixel-level numbers behind these rules live in Visual/Audio Requirements.

**DA-40 The CONTENT lint table.** (registry: `content_lint_ids`, 74 rows) Rows are data: `{lint_id, severity, run point, reads, rule}`, so Procedural Derelict Generation consumes the same table. Run points: **BOOT** (the six-rule subset of DA-41), **CI** (the offline and continuous-integration run), **ASSET** (`tools/asset-pipeline/` over sheets, not templates). Layout's own ids are cited and never redefined: `ROOM_NO_EXIT`, `ROOM_UNREACHABLE`, `CLEAR_PATH_BLOCKED`, `STORY_SLOT_LIST`, `CORRIDOR_HEAD_NARROW`, `SOUTH_BAND_OCCUPIED`, `OUTER_ROOM_HULL_RUNS`, `SEGMENT_TILE_SHARED`, `HATCH_HINGE_AUTHORED`, `DOOR_POCKET_CLEAR`, `DOOR_APPROACH_CLEAR`, `LOOSE_ON_DOOR`, `WALL_FITTING_NO_RUN`, `FLOOR_RUN_CROSSES_BOUNDARY`, `ROOM_EXCEEDS_FRAME`, `V_RANGE`, `EXPOSED_RANGE`.

**DA-41 Lint run points, the one config file and determinism.** The full content lint runs offline and in CI (the project's gdUnit4 command line; the `tests/` tree and `addons/gdUnit4` do not exist yet, IDB OQ 28, so this is stated as the target). At BOOTING the game re-checks only six rules: `PLACEMENT_DEF_UNKNOWN`, `SELF_POWERED_LAMP_COUNT` (EM:665: the authoring lint and boot refuse any other self-powered fixture), `BEACON_COUNT`, `STORY_SLOT_LIST` (Layout's), `RESIDUAL_LIGHT_COUNT` and `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED`. The structural lint is Layout's BOOTING pass and is cited, never re-listed. A failed CI content lint never bricks a release boot beyond that subset. The lint is a **pure function of the files plus one named config**: every threshold it reads lives in `content_lint_config`, rows `{name, value, owner, status in {LOCKED, PLACEHOLDER}}`; `TUNING_SOURCE_DRIFT` compares each LOCKED row with the registry and each PLACEHOLDER row with the owner GDD's current text, so a placeholder cannot silently become a fact. The PLACEHOLDER rows this GDD reads from other owners (each a `content_lint_config` row with status PLACEHOLDER, never LOCKED) include `MAX_EMITTERS` (Emitter), `BEACON_SWEEP_TICKS` (Emitter), `LANTERN_LIGHTS_MAX`, `NONSHADOW_LIGHTS_MAX`, `SHADOW_SLOTS_MAX` and `OCCLUDER_POLYS_VIEW_MAX` (Lighting) and `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` (Item DB), beside the rows already so marked in the text. Output is ordered by `(template_id, lint_id, record_key)` ascending and two runs over the same bytes produce identical bytes; waivers are rows `{lint_id, record_key, reason, owner}` in the same file, a waiver without a reason is an ERROR, and `frame_exception` and the PD-1 exceptions are waivers of this form (no waiver may name `STORY_SLOT_FILLED_IN_MVP`). -> ADR candidate: the lint runner's shape, language and location.

**DA-42 Content acceptance runs as headless tests, not lint.** Rules that need the Graph to run are integration tests in `tests/integration/derelict_authoring/` using the kernel's headless path (H10): `G0_ZERO_PART_SURVIVABLE` (IDB AC-42: a patch-only run of all eight faults ends none FAILED or DESTROYED, including the case "`v_o2_cryo` never opened", which the closed-valve staging fails until ASK-G1 lands and the valve-OPEN fallback passes, DA-16), `FAULT_D15_DRIFT` (each live fault within +-15 % of the Graph's D.15 at its trigger), `DEAD_FAULT_STILL_REPAIRABLE` (every dead fault is still repairable at each loaded idle time below its D.14 `T_destroy` and, once ASK-G1 gates wear, after `T_SORTIE_LOADED_MAX` minutes of loaded idle; under the literal reading the test asserts the ledger itself: `pb_leak` repairable at 15.0 min and DESTROYED at 15.3 min of B-loaded idle), `WELD_WITHOUT_PLATE_SURVIVABLE` (welding B with no plate and patching only keeps `seg_b_pump_n` from staying below `BREACH_HP` for more than 60 s at a 3.5-minute patch cadence) and `SCRAM_RESTART_STEADY` (patch `pb_leak`, then V12: `r_b` reaches RUNNING and does not re-SCRAM in 10 minutes; skip the patch and it SCRAMs again within 90 s +- 15 s). The harness shape is an ADR candidate; they are tagged (auto) in Acceptance Criteria.

**DA-43 The authoring workflow (data first; editor tooling deferred).** (1) The floor plan is drawn as an ASCII grid in `tools/asset-pipeline/plans/<template_id>.txt` (one character per 48 px tile, half-tile walls on the 24 px lattice per LY-3). (2) A converter turns the grid into `tile_runs`, wall runs and the rect table. (3) Placements, nodes, fixtures, faults and ledgers are hand-written records. (4) The offline lint runs Layout's structural rules, then this GDD's CONTENT rules. (5) The template is written to `assets/data/sections/`. A Godot editor exporter (an `@tool` or EditorPlugin route) is deferred to an ADR and a spike. **Engine feasibility:** the pinned reference has no scripting, core, editor or tools module (`docs/engine-reference/godot/modules/` covers animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering and ui only), so every claim about JSON loading, editor tooling, TileMapLayer authoring and export filters is `[UNV]`: `JSON.parse_string` returning floats (cast and bound-check every authored integer), the export include filter for `assets/data/**/*.json` (a 30-minute spike against the 4.7.2 export templates, IDB OQ 23) and a 4.7 typed-return override rule for any loader or lint base class are the items to verify. -> ADR candidates: authoring tool pipeline and the offline lint runner; headless content harness; worn-atlas derivation tool; atlas selection by `template_id` in the PROJECTOR. This GDD describes behaviour and data shape, never code.

**DA-44 Template edits and registry discipline.** A template edit changes its `template_hash` (LY-26 drift policy); saves before the first external build are disposable (SL-14) and template migration begins only after it. A `room_key` change is committed only with a note in the file header. Registry discipline: owner-named values that are not registry entries (`ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX`, `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, `MAX_STORY_SLOTS_PER_SECTION`, `DOOR_CORNER_MARGIN_PX`, `TILES_WORLD_MVP`, `SOCKET_SPACING_MIN_TILES`) are cited by owner GDD and rule and never minted here; `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` (Interaction) and `part_cover_pm` (Item DB F-11, with its band as variables) are registry entries and are cited by name and registry value; `FREEZE_ON_MC` -20000 is a registry entry (Graph-owned), as are `LEAK_HP` and `BREACH_HP` 4000. The final stage registers only this GDD's own artefacts, from the final text: the template roster (`template_id` values) (registry: `mvp_template_roster`), the `visible_unknown` record, the MVP fixture-count table (citing LY D.16) (registry: `mvp_fixture_counts`), the fault-allocation table (registry: `mvp_fault_allocation`), the MVP ledger instance (citing IDB F-11) (registry: `mvp_part_ledger`), the legal loaded sets of DA-32 (registry: `legal_loaded_sets`), the content lint id list (registry: `content_lint_ids`), the kit-manifest schema name (registry: `kit_manifest_schema`) and the `_worn` token (registry: `worn_variant_token`); the 36 `pending referenced_by` comments become real `referenced_by` entries. (`RESERVE_S` 90 and `INSUL_MC` 5000 were registered by Suit & Equipment after this list was first drawn; they are cited as registry entries, with Suit's placeholder tags kept at each mention.)

**DA-45 The first ten minutes are content-shaped (MVP onboarding).** MVP onboarding is the cryo-bay fixture and a hand-led sequence the layout supports; there is no scripted tutorial and no marker. The player wakes with one guttering EMERGENCY lamp; closes the cryo breaker `j_cryo` (V3); the room warms at about 4 mC per Graph step gross (500 W into C = 42 x `C_TILE` 3000 = 126,000 dJ/°C, LSG D.7), about 3 mC net of hull loss at 13 exposed tiles, so about 6.5 minutes (about 3,900 Graph steps) from -2000 mC to COMFORT at 10000 mC; LSG:839's 2.5 minutes is its 20-tile example (D.18); follows the frost trail from `p_cryo_rad` to the failed fitting; patches it (V1). The cryo-bay door opens onto a lit corridor with the beacon dark; the engine-room door `door_engine_n` stands open (DA-16) and the ember glow of the Fuel Ember row at the `pf_feed` flange is the next thing in view. Later onboarding (Vertical Slice) replaces the hand lead with the Onboarding system.



### States and Transitions

Authoring has **no runtime state** (DA-1): nothing here is a state block and nothing is hashed by Authoring. The states below are (1) the lifecycle of a template as a content artefact and (2) the states of authored subjects whose *initial* value this GDD writes and whose *transitions* belong to the owner named in the last column. The point of listing them is to say who may cause each change and to make every authored initial state one of the values the owner already defines.

**Template lifecycle (content states).**

| State | Entry condition | Exit condition | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TEMPLATE_DRAFT` | a plan file `tools/asset-pipeline/plans/<template_id>.txt` exists, or any edit to a shipped template | the converter runs without error | to `TEMPLATE_CONVERTED` | the level-designer (author) |
| `TEMPLATE_CONVERTED` | `tile_runs`, wall runs and the rect table are produced | Layout's structural lint reports zero ERROR | to `TEMPLATE_LINTED`; back to `TEMPLATE_DRAFT` on any ERROR | the converter; Layout's lint (CI) |
| `TEMPLATE_LINTED` | the structural lint passes | the full CONTENT lint (DA-40) reports zero ERROR | to `TEMPLATE_CONTENT_CLEAN`; back to draft on any ERROR | the CONTENT lint (CI) |
| `TEMPLATE_CONTENT_CLEAN` | the CONTENT lint passes (WARNs listed, not blocking) | the six-rule BOOT subset (DA-41) passes inside a build | to `TEMPLATE_SHIPPED` | the build / CI |
| `TEMPLATE_SHIPPED` | the file is in `assets/data/sections/` and its `template_hash` is recorded | any edit | to `TEMPLATE_DRAFT` (new `template_hash`; saves before the first external build are disposable, SL-14) | the author only; never at runtime (LY-2) |

**Authored subjects (initial state written here; transitions owned elsewhere).**

| Subject | State | Entry condition | Exit condition | Transitions | Who may cause them |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fault (live or dead) | `LATENT` | held by a lever at build: a closed valve (flow only, DA-16), an open breaker, a thin margin, an absent load, or an absent weld | the lever is released | to `ACTIVE` when the player or the weld releases it; PWR-OVL on installing a heater; O2-ROOM on the weld | the player (V3, V5, install, V9); Assembly (the weld) |
| Fault | `DRIED` | unserved at build, `intensity_pm` 0, `since_steps` at least `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` `[PLACEHOLDER: Failure & Trail]` | power or coolant reaches it | to `ACTIVE`; a non-leaky dead fault is otherwise stable, a leaky one wears (DA-17) | the player feeding the branch; the Graph's wear |
| Fault | `ACTIVE` | `intensity_pm` above 0; joins the glow count | the Graph's mode machine | to the Graph's DEGRADED, FAILED, DESTROYED, PATCHED states (the Graph's, not Authoring's) | the Graph (endogenous); the player (patch, repair, close a valve) |
| Residual lamp | `LIT` | island battery stock above 0, lamp `switch_on` true | stock reaches 0 | to `DRAINED` while the section is loaded; the lamp dims for one step and goes dark, it never fades for effect | the Graph (D.6 draw); a Suit top-up withdrawal shortens it step for step |
| Residual lamp | `DRAINED` | battery stock 0 | the tie is closed on a powered bus | to `CHARGING`; otherwise none | the player closing the tie junction (V3) with the bus served |
| Residual lamp | `CHARGING` | tie closed, bus served | stock at its cap, or the tie opens | back to `LIT` as stock rises; recharge at `min(S - served, CH_CAP)` per step (D.6) | the player; the Graph |
| Wreck bus (A, B) | `DEAD` | build state: junction open or broken (A), reactor SCRAMmed (B) | the junction is replaced or patched and closed (A); V12 restarts the reactor (B) | to `WOKEN`: lanterns come on, dormant faults emit, the DERELICT clock applies to the loaded section | the player (swap breaker, V12, V3) |
| Door | `CLOSED` / `OPEN` | `area` 0 or `area_max` at build per the room tables | the player or an opening event | between the two (the Graph's `area` and `door_state`, Layout's leaf presentation); `cut` and `welded` leaf states are not used on interior doors in MVP (DA-8) | Interaction (`set_opening_area`, M13); the Graph |
| Socket | `FREE` | build state (the only MVP sockets are `sock_airlock_e`, `sock_b_w`) | the weld | `FREE` to `WELDED` to `CAPPED` or `CUT_OPEN`, never back (Layout's `socket_state`) | Assembly's atomic apply, through Layout's LM2 `attach_section` and LM3 `detach_section` (Assembly is on disk, In Design) |
| Section load | `LOADED` / `DORMANT` | built and loaded / not loaded | load or unload | the Graph skips a dormant section's slots (LS-17); `can_join` returns `SECTION_NOT_LOADED` | Web Map & Transit (provisional — undesigned); not Authoring |
| Loose item | `PLACED` | seeded by `place_loose` at world build | picked up | to carried (Interaction's holder) and back by a drop | Interaction; Authoring owns only the initial placement |
| Module node | at its authored `hp` | built | cut free, installed, patched, repaired, destroyed | the Graph and Interaction's modules lifecycle (M6, M7, M5) | the player; the Graph |

A section's template state, a body's pose and a fault's mode are three different things: this GDD writes the first and the *initial* value of the last, and owns neither a transition nor a hash.


### Interactions with Other Systems

**Authoring registers no hook, no state block, no mutator and no read_view (DA-1).** Every interface below is the consumer's or the provider's; `data in` means what Authoring reads to write or lint its files, `data out` means what Authoring's files supply. Disk status (Glob of `design/gdd/`, 2026-10-26, at the time this part was written): designed — kernel, Graph, Item DB, Controller, Pipeline, Emitter, 2D Lighting, Failure & Trail, Save/Load, Suit, Interaction, Ship Section & Layout, Ship Body & Interior Physics, Attention; **on disk, In Design (refreshed 2026-10-27)** — Modular Ship Assembly (159 KB; it names this GDD 14 times and its C11 flags the Assembly row below as wrong on ownership, now corrected); **on disk since 2026-10-26 (rows refreshed 2026-10-27 where an ask arrived)** — Boarding & Salvage, Web Map & Transit, Horror Entities, Sanity, Perception Filter, In-World Instruments, Chart Table, Adaptive Audio, HUD, Debug Overlay; **no file** — Procedural Derelict Generation, Environmental Storytelling, Onboarding. Every contract with a system in the last group is marked `(provisional — X undesigned/in flight)` and says what that GDD must repeat.

## Formulas

**Conventions.** Authoring has no runtime math of its own (DA-1): every formula below is either a **lint formula** (a pure function of the authored files plus `content_lint_config`, DA-41), a **derivation** that fixes an authored number from a Graph, Item DB or Layout formula (so the number is solved, not guessed), or the **closed form a headless test asserts** (DA-42). All inputs are authored integers. A Graph step is `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 ticks = 0.1 s, so 600 steps = 1 minute; watts are dJ per step (LSG LS-1). Formula ids `D.1` to `D.21` are fixed by the index in Core Rules and are never renumbered; the Graph's formulas are always written `LSG D.n` (the Graph's own D.15 is the fixture table, this GDD's D.15 is the patch and fatigue treadmill). Nothing here is hashed or replicated; the only transcendental function (D.18's natural logarithm) is lint-side and forbidden on the sim path (LSG LS-1 g), and the lint evaluates it with a fixed-point table so two runs over the same bytes print identical bytes (DA-41). Values resting on a provisional upstream carry `[PLACEHOLDER: owner]` at every mention.

### D.1 Tile volume budget per reachable set

The `tile_volume_budget` formula is defined as:

`world_tiles(S) = sum over every room r of every section in S of V(r) + POD_TILES x pods_loaded(S)` ; `ERROR iff world_tiles(S) > TILES_WORLD_MVP` ; `WARN iff 100 x world_tiles(S) >= WORLD_TILES_WARN_PCT x TILES_WORLD_MVP`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V(r)` | int tiles | 4 to 200 | LY D.3 (`room_volume`) | Volume of one compartment; the room tables of C.1 to C.3 are the authored values |
| `S` | set of sections | the five reachable sets of DA-32 | DA-32 `[provisional: Web Map & Transit undesigned]` | The sections loaded at the same time |
| `POD_TILES` | int tiles | 4 | DA-3 (`pod_cabin` `V` 4) | Added when the persistent pod body exists (always dormant, WM-5); a pod is not in the three-ship totals |
| `pods_loaded(S)` | int | 0 or 1 | Web Map & Transit (registry `pod_body`) | Pod bodies in the world (0 before `init_world`, 1 after; the 453 figure includes it) |
| `TILES_WORLD_MVP` | int tiles | 600 | LY D.13 (a Layout knob citing FT:598; not a registry entry) | World tile budget |
| `WORLD_TILES_WARN_PCT` | int % | 90 | LY D.13 | Warning fraction (WARN at 540 tiles) |

**Output Range:** 0 to `TILES_WORLD_MVP` + 4; the MVP content spans 182 (`{home}`) to 453 (all three ships plus a docked pod). Per-section bounds are Layout's (`SECTION_MAX_TILES_W` 48 by `SECTION_MAX_TILES_H` 32, `MAX_ROOMS_PER_SECTION` 8); the authored `size_tiles` are 16 x 18, 17 x 14 and 20 x 18.
**Example:** `home_ship` 42 + 39 + 36 + 35 + 30 = 182; `derelict_a` 20 + 33 + 30 + 35 = 118; `derelict_b` 20 + 39 + 40 + 20 + 30 = 149. S0 `{home}` 182 (30.3 %); S1 `{home, A}` 300 (50.0 %); S2 and S3 `{home, B}` 331 (55.2 %); S4 `{home+B, A}` 449 (74.8 %); S4 with a docked pod 453 (75.5 %). Every set sits 87 tiles or more under the 540 WARN line, so `TILE_BUDGET` is silent at MVP and tracks the budget for the Vertical Slice.

### D.2 Count tolerance (one-sided)

The `count_tolerance` formula is defined as:

`over = authored - target` ; `tol = max(floor(target x NODE_DRIFT_PCT / 100), NODE_DRIFT_ABS_MIN)` ; `NODE_COUNT_DRIFT = WARN iff over > tol` ; `INFO iff over < 0` ; `ERROR iff sum of authored nodes over the world > NODE_HARD_MAX`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `authored` | int | 0 to 120 | the templates (DA-19 ledger) | Count of nodes, design edges or LOAD nodes for one section |
| `target` | int | the "about" counts of LSG LS-26 | LSG LS-26 (LSG:228) | Nodes about 40 / 30 / 35 (home / A / B); edges about 55 (home) and about 45 (the B cost row of LSG D.16); LOADs about 8 (home). LS-26 gives A no edge or LOAD target |
| `NODE_DRIFT_PCT` | int % | 10 to 30 (20) | Authoring knob | Relative tolerance |
| `NODE_DRIFT_ABS_MIN` | int | 2 to 5 (3) | Authoring knob | Absolute floor so a small target is not a hair-trigger |
| `NODE_HARD_MAX` | int | 120 | LSG:787 (the Graph's MVP content bound; `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 and the 800-node WARN of LSG:915 are irrelevant at this scale) | Hard cap on authored nodes in the world |

**Output Range:** an INFO, a WARN or a pass per count; below target is never a defect, because the Graph's cost model (LSG D.16) is monotone in count.
**Example:** home nodes 42 against 40: over 2, `tol` max(8, 3) = 8, pass. A nodes 27 against 30: over -3, INFO. B nodes 35 against 35: over 0. World nodes 104 against 120: pass. Home LOADs 11 against 8: over 3, `tol` max(floor(1.6), 3) = 3; the lint warns only when over **exceeds** `tol`, so 3 passes with no margin (one more lamp or motor on `home_ship` fires the WARN). Home design edges 34 against about 55: over -21, INFO (a tree of n nodes needs n - 1 edges, DA-9, so 34 is the structural minimum for this node set, DA-19); B edges 29 against about 45: INFO. Per-kind edge targets in LS-26 ("about 8 CABLEs", "about 5 DUCTs", "3 PIPEs") are reported as INFO and never linted: the home power tree has 16 CABLE edges because 17 power nodes need 16 (DA-20), twice LS-26's "about 8", which is flagged in Edge Cases and Open Questions as a wording tension with the Graph's estimate, not a defect.

### D.3 Fixture bound use (Layout D.16)

The `fixture_bound_use` formula is defined as:

`fixtures_max(V) = min(6, 1 + floor_div(V, 8))` ; `room_ok = (FIXTURES_MIN <= n_fix <= fixtures_max(V))` ; `set_ok(S) = (sum over rooms in S of n_fix <= FIXTURE_WORLD_MAX)` ; `emitter_tally(S) = fixtures(S) + lanterns(S) + beacons(S) + glow_bound(S) <= MAX_EMITTERS`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `V` | int tiles | 4 to 200 | LY D.3 | Room volume |
| `n_fix` | int | 1 to 6 | DA-22 tables | Authored fixtures (lamp housings, lit or dark) in the room |
| `FIXTURES_MIN` | int | 1 | LY D.16 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter AC-58]` | A dark housing still counts (EM-13) |
| `FIXTURE_WORLD_MAX` | int | 28 | EM AC-58 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` | Fixtures per loaded set |
| `lanterns(S)`, `beacons(S)` | int | 0 to 8 / 0 to 1 | DA-24 | Authored NODE_LANTERNs and BEACONs in the set |
| `glow_bound(S)` | int | 0 to 10 | D.12 | Fault-glow rows bound for the body |
| `MAX_EMITTERS` `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` | int | 96 (registry); the Emitter derives 70 for authored content | registry; EM AC-58 | Emitter cap |

**Output Range:** `fixtures_max` is 1 (V below 8) to 6 (V of 40 or more). The per-set sum is 8 to 21 in MVP content.
**Example:** `cryo_bay` V 42: `min(6, 1 + 5)` = 6, authored 2, pass. `b_pump` V 20: `min(6, 1 + 2)` = 3, authored 1, pass. `airlock` V 30: 4, authored 1. `b_reactor` V 40: 6, authored 2. Maxima by room: home 6, 5, 5, 5, 4; A 3, 5, 4, 5; B 3, 5, 6, 3, 4. Set sums: S0 8, S1 14, S2 and S3 15, S4 21, all at or under 28. Emitter tally for S4: 21 fixtures + 8 lanterns + 1 beacon + at most 10 glow rows = 40 authored, at most the Emitter's derived 70.

### D.4 Part cover use and access order (Item DB F-11)

The `part_cover_use` formula is defined as:

`part_cover_pm = floor(1000 x parts_supplied / full_repairs_demanded)` ; `in_band = (PART_COVER_MIN_PM <= part_cover_pm <= PART_COVER_MAX_PM)` ; `access_order(part) = the first sortie set whose rooms contain the part's slot`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `parts_supplied` | int | 0 to 7 | DA-25 ledger (loose-part placements + DESTROYED module nodes at build) | A 5-part ledger: A 2, B 3, none on `home_ship` |
| `full_repairs_demanded` | int | 7 (fixed by the list) | IDB F-11 | The pinhole DUCT, the O2 seam, the overloaded CABLE, the broken CABLE_JUNCTION, the coolant PIPE, the fuel PIPE, the fatigued HULL_SEGMENT |
| `PART_COVER_MIN_PM`, `PART_COVER_MAX_PM` | int permille | 500 / 800 | IDB F-11 (advisory band; variables of registry formula `part_cover_pm`) | `PART_COVER_BAND` is a WARN |

**Output Range:** 0 to 1000 and above; the MVP value is 714, inside the band.
**Example:** floor(1000 x 5 / 7) = 714, pass. Supply 3 gives 428 (hopeless), 4 gives 571, 6 gives 857 (a WARN above 800: every chronic fault solved), 7 gives 1000. Family cover: pipe 2 of 2, hull 1 of 1, duct 1 of 2, cable 1 of 2; uncovered by choice: the O2 seam and the broken junction (the spare breaker covers the junction in kind). Access order: `part_hull_plate` (A, `a_spine`) and `part_cable_splice` (A, `a_machine`, inside the lethal cold room) are reached on the first sortie; both `part_pipe_fitting` (B) and `part_duct_coupling` (B, across the vacuum) on the second. The first fault of the game (the cryo-bay coolant pipe) therefore cannot be fully repaired until the second sortie, about 45 to 55 minutes in (a pacing estimate: sortie A 15 to 20 minutes, sortie B 20 to 25, travel), which at the D.15 patch cadence is about 20 patch actions on the home leaks; that scarcity is deliberate (P5) and G-0 holds throughout.

### D.5 Escape-route use, run, walk and carry (Layout D.19)

The `escape_route_use` formula is defined as:

`budget_px(v) = W_MIN_WARNING_S x TICK_RATE x v` ; `ESCAPE_ROUTE_OVER_BUDGET iff min over closable doors d of route_length_px(anchor, d) > budget_px(V_RUN)` ; `walk_ticks = ceil(route_length_px / v)` (the `n_closed = 0` term of Boarding & Salvage's `route_ticks`) ; `escape_slack_ticks = W_MIN_WARNING_S x TICK_RATE - walk_ticks - door_close_ticks`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_MIN_WARNING_S` | int s | 10 | registry | The fairness window (endogenous transitions only, LSG LS-13) |
| `TICK_RATE` | int | 60 | registry | Ticks per second |
| `v` | int px per tick | `V_RUN` 3, `V_WALK` 2, `V_CARRY` 1 | registry | Run, walk, carry speeds |
| `route_length_px` | int px | 0 to the section's span | Layout read (LY D.19; `ESCAPE_ROUTE_BUDGET_PX` is Layout's, not a registry entry) | Path length from a fault anchor to a closable door |
| `door_close_ticks` | int | `[UNV: Interaction]` | Interaction door channel | Taken as 0 by the lint, reported as unverified |
| `walk_ticks` | int ticks | 0 to 600 at the budget | derived | Travel time from anchor to door, `ceil(route_length_px / v)`; the `n_closed = 0` term of Boarding & Salvage's registry formula `route_ticks`, which this GDD does not redefine |

**Output Range:** `budget_px` is 1,800 at `V_RUN`, 1,200 at `V_WALK`, 600 at `V_CARRY`; `escape_slack_ticks` runs up to 600 (10 s) and is zero at the budget.
**Example:** `pf_feed` to `door_engine_n`, about 480 px (Layout's read governs): `walk_ticks` running 160 (2.7 s), slack 440 ticks (7.3 s); walking 240 ticks, slack 360; carrying 480 ticks, slack 120 ticks (2.0 s). `p_cryo_rad` to `door_cryo_s`, about 336 px: running 112 ticks (1.9 s). `seg_b_pump_n` to `door_b_spine_pump`, about 384 px: running 128 ticks (2.1 s). The longest, 480 px, is 27 % of the 1,800 budget. A route over 600 px is legal on foot and fails only `ESCAPE_ROUTE_CARRY` (WARN) when a salvageable module node (a module in hand moves at `V_CARRY` 1, PC-8) shares the room with a live endogenous fault; a stowed part, the 30 kg plate included, does not change speed (IDB:280, SE-28).

### D.6 Residual-light clock, recharge and withdrawal

The `residual_light_clock` formula is defined as:

`t_lit_steps = floor(charge_dJ / req_w)` ; `t_lit_withdrawn = floor((charge_dJ - withdrawn_dJ) / req_w)` ; `t_recharge_steps = ceil((BATT_CELL - stock) / min(S - sum_served, CH_CAP))` ; `exterior_lit = (lamp node state in {OK, DEGRADED} and persisted island stock > 0)` for a built wreck, `(charge_dJ > 0)` for a never-built one

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `charge_dJ` | int dJ | 180,000 to 1,440,000 | authored: `b_a1` 540,000, `b_b1` 432,000 (DA-27); BATTERY `stock` | Island battery stock at build; never a percent (DA-4) |
| `req_w` | int W = dJ per step | 20 (fixed) | EM-11; `mod_lamp` | The lamp is the island's only load |
| `BATT_CELL` | int dJ | 3,600,000 | LSG D.6 variable table; IDB:220 | A full cell; only the derivation base |
| `withdrawn_dJ` | int dJ | 0 to `charge_dJ` | Suit's top-up (SE) | Stock removed by a `withdraw_store` |
| `S`, `sum_served`, `CH_CAP` | int W | S 0 to 10000; `CH_CAP` 0 to 500 (300) | LSG D.6 | Source output, served power, recharge cap (after the tie is closed on a powered bus) |

**Output Range:** `t_lit_steps` 9,000 to 72,000 for stocks of 180,000 to 1,440,000 dJ; the clock counts **loaded** time only, because an unloaded section's slots are skipped (LSG LS-17).
**Example:** A: 540,000 / 20 = 27,000 steps = 2,700 s = **45.0 minutes** of loaded time; B: 432,000 / 20 = 21,600 steps = **36.0 minutes**. A Suit top-up that withdraws 100,000 dJ from `b_a1` leaves floor(440,000 / 20) = 22,000 steps (36.7 minutes). Recharge on B after V12 and a closed `j_b_res` with surplus of at least 300 W: ceil(3,168,000 / 300) = 10,560 steps = 17.6 minutes to a full cell. The last step is a one-step dim and then dark, never a fade for effect (the lamp is steady, unmodulated, DA-27). The exterior rule is the Web Map & Transit contract `[provisional: Web Map & Transit undesigned]`.

### D.7 Traverse bound use (Suit D.11)

The `traverse_bound_use` formula is defined as:

`traverse_s = tiles x TILE_PX / (v x TICK_RATE) <= RESERVE_S / 2` (Suit's, one-way, verbatim) ; `TRAVERSE_CARRY_MAX_TILES = floor((RESERVE_S / 2) x TICK_RATE x V_CARRY / TILE_PX)` ; `TRAVERSE_TOO_LONG iff 2 x tiles_one_way (summed over nested unbreathable rooms) > TRAVERSE_CARRY_MAX_TILES`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tiles` | int | 0 to 112 | the authored path across the unbreathable compartment | Path length in tiles (a Manhattan bound, width plus depth, for a room) |
| `v` | int px per tick | `V_WALK` 2 or `V_CARRY` 1 | registry | Speed on foot or carrying a module |
| `TILE_PX` | int px | 48 | registry | Tile size |
| `RESERVE_S` | int s | 90 | registry (Suit-owned, `[PLACEHOLDER: Suit]`); SE D.11 | The suit's vacuum or cold reserve |

**Output Range:** 112 tiles one way walking and 56 tiles carrying at the defaults (112 x 48 / 120 = 44.8 s; 56 x 48 / 60 = 44.8 s, both at or under 45 s). Authoring applies the carry bound to the **round trip** (R17, "and back"), which is stricter than Suit's own one-way text; the lint prints Suit's figure beside it (DA-28).
**Example:** `b_breach` (6 x 5): about 11 tiles each way, a 22-tile round trip, carry time 22 x 48 = 1,056 ticks = 17.6 s. When `b_pump` (4 x 5, 9 tiles each way) is itself decompressed the worst case adds 18: 22 + 18 = 40 of 56, pass, with 16 tiles of slack. A hypothetical 30-tile one-way vacuum corridor would fail (60 over 56) while passing Suit's own walking bound, which is the intended extra caution.

### D.8 Containment colour count (Lighting LO-33, PD-1)

The `containment_colour_count` formula is defined as:

`edge(r1, r2) iff gap_px(r1, r2) <= R_DRAW_MAX_PX + WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` ; `colours_needed = colours used by greedy colouring of the proximity graph in room-table order` ; `CONTAIN_COLOUR_REPORT = ERROR iff colours_needed > CONTAIN_COLOURS`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gap_px` | int px | 0 upward | Layout's room rects | Euclidean distance between the closest points of two room rectangles |
| `R_DRAW_MAX_PX` | int px | 122 | Lighting (LO) | Longest light draw reach |
| `WALL_FOOTPRINT_DEPTH` | int px | 24 | registry | Wall strip depth; the edge radius is 146 px |
| `CONTAIN_COLOURS` | int | 4 `[PLACEHOLDER: Lighting]` | registry | Available containment colours |

**Output Range:** 1 to `CONTAIN_COLOURS` for a legal body; a larger value is a CONFLICT paragraph to Lighting, never a re-shaped room (DA-31). The proximity edge is geometric, so the count is the same with every door open and every door closed; the lint runs and prints both.
**Example:** home edges: cryo-corridor, cryo-cargo, corridor-cargo, corridor-engine, corridor-airlock, engine-airlock (all 24 px gaps); cargo-airlock and cryo-engine are 192 px, no edge. Greedy colours: cryo 1, corridor 2, cargo 3, engine 1, airlock 3 = **3**. Derelict A: the clique `a_spine`, `a_life`, `a_machine` = **3**. Derelict B alone: the cliques `b_spine`, `b_reactor`, `b_pump` and `b_reactor`, `b_pump`, `b_breach` (the 72 px exterior notch leaves a 120 px gap, inside 146) = **3**. Home + B after the weld at offset (696, 384): `b_dock` is 48 px from `airlock` and a diagonal about 76 px from the corridor (corner (672, 552) to (744, 576): dx 72, dy 24, still inside 146), so it takes colour 1, and the body still needs **3**. 3 of `CONTAIN_COLOURS` 4: margin 1. Two of the 14 rooms (`spine_corridor`, `b_pump`) are PD-1 exceptions under Lighting's rewritten per-face C3 (DA-31).

### D.9 PWR-OVL window, trigger and trunk cap

The `pwr_ovl_window` formula is defined as:

`rho_pm = floor(1000 x L_below / cap_W)` ; `dhp_ovl = min(DHP_MAX, floor(OVL_K x max(0, rho_pm - 1000)^2 / 10^4))` per step ; `cap_lo(L) = floor(1000 x L / (OVL_WINDOW_HI_PM + 1)) + 1` ; `cap_hi(L) = floor(1000 x L / OVL_WINDOW_LO_PM)` ; `legal_cap = [max over beacon states of cap_lo, min over beacon states of cap_hi]` ; `OVERLOAD_WINDOW iff OVL_WINDOW_LO_PM <= rho_pm(trigger, beacon off) and rho_pm(trigger, beacon on) <= OVL_WINDOW_HI_PM`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `L_below` | int W | 0 to `S` + `D` | the published power tree (DA-20); LSG D.6 derived line (served power in the subtree below the cable) | Served load under `cab_trunk`, the root edge above every LOAD |
| `cap_W` | int W | 500 to 6000 | authored `cap` of `cab_trunk` | **2161 W** |
| `OVL_K`, `DHP_MAX` | int | 4 / 2000 | LSG D.9 | Overload scale and clamp |
| `OVL_WINDOW_LO_PM`, `OVL_WINDOW_HI_PM` | int permille | 1100 / 1111 | derived here from LSG D.15's PWR-OVL band | The `rho_pm` interval in which `dhp_ovl` is exactly 4: `(rho_pm - 1000)^2` in [10000, 12499]; 3 gives 3,334 steps and 5 gives 2,000, both outside 2,500 +-15 % |
| trigger `L` | int W | 2380 (beacon off), 2400 (beacon on) | DA-20 | Standing 1880 W (1900 with the beacon lamp lit) plus one installed `mod_heater` (500 W) |
| `S` | int W | 2500 `[PLACEHOLDER: Item DB out_w_rated]` | LSG D.6 | Reactor supply that clamps served power |

**Output Range:** `dhp_ovl` is 0 at `rho_pm` of 1000 or below, 4 inside the window, up to `DHP_MAX` 2000. The legal `cap` for a 500 W trigger over the 1880 W standing load is cap_lo(2380) = 2141 to cap_hi(2380) = 2163 with the beacon off and cap_lo(2400) = 2159 to cap_hi(2400) = 2181 with it on: the intersection is **2159 to 2163 W**, and 2161 sits in the middle. Both limits are solved, not tuned (a 2150 W cap fails the beacon-on state, rho 1116 and `dhp_ovl` 5; a 2000 W cap gives rho 1190 and `dhp_ovl` 14, DESTROYED in 71 s).
**Example:** at `cap` 2161 W the standing load 1880 W gives `rho_pm` 869 (879 with the beacon lit, 573 at wake with `j_cryo` open), no damage. The trigger, an installed `htr_a`, gives L 2380, `rho_pm` 1101, `dhp_ovl` floor(4 x 101^2 / 10^4) = 4; with the beacon on, L 2400, `rho_pm` 1110, `dhp_ovl` floor(4 x 110^2 / 10^4) = 4. Timeline at 4 bp per step from 10000: `hp` below `HP_OK` 7000 after 751 steps (75 s, DEGRADED, the tell starts), at or below `HP_FAIL` 3000 after 1,750 steps (2.9 minutes, FAILED), 0 after 2,500 steps (4.2 minutes, DESTROYED), reproducing LSG D.15's PWR-OVL row (4.5 minutes, 3.8 to 5.2). The fairness gate never engages: 1,750 - 751 = 999 steps against `W_MIN_STEPS` 100. Other adds at `cap` 2161: a lamp (1900 W, rho 879) and a door motor (2000 W, rho 925) are safe; a `mod_coolant_pump` (2280 W, rho 1055, 1 bp per step) is DESTROYED in 10,000 steps (16.7 minutes); a `mod_scrubber` (2480 W, rho 1147, 8 bp per step) in 1,250 steps (2.1 minutes), a harsher player mistake that is outside the authored fixture by design; any larger add is supply-clamped to S = 2500 W, rho 1156, 9 bp per step, 1,112 steps (1.9 minutes) at least (battery discharge `D` can add more, `[UNV: Graph discharge cap]`). Inrush on the first V3: the cryo branch (640 W) requests floor(640 x 1500 / 1000) = 960 W for `INRUSH_STEPS` 5, trunk L 2200, rho 1018, `dhp_ovl` 0. A blackout recovery that re-energises everything at once is supply-clamped to rho 1156 for 5 steps, 9 x 5 = 45 bp of scorch per recovery. **Welded body:** closing the seam breaker on a SCRAMmed `r_b` loads the trunk to 1880 + 1240 = 3120 W, supply-clamped to S 2500 W, rho 1156, 9 bp per step, DESTROYED (`NOT_REPAIRABLE`) in 1,112 steps (about 1.9 minutes), the same line as the clamped adds above; the rule and its tell are in DA-20.

### D.10 Cold-room exposure budget (the lethal cold room)

The `cold_room_exposure` formula is defined as (Suit D.4 used verbatim, not restated; the Authoring-side use is the margin check):

`e = onset_cold - T` ; `gain = TL_GAIN_MIN + (TL_GAIN_MAX - TL_GAIN_MIN) x min(e, TL_DEPTH_CAP_MC) / TL_DEPTH_CAP_MC` (integer) ; `runs_critical = ceil(TL_CRITICAL / gain)` ; `runs_failed = ceil(TL_MAX / gain)` ; `cross_ticks = depth_tiles x TILE_PX / V_CARRY` ; `equilibrium = T_AMB_MC + ceil(100000 / (K_HULL x S_ext))`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `onset_cold` | int mC | -20000 | `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` -15000 less `INSUL_MC` 5000 `[PLACEHOLDER: Suit]` (SE D.4; LSG:1040) | Shifted cold-damage onset, at full suit integrity (at or above `SUIT_LEAK_HP`); a leaking suit moves the onset toward `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` -15000 (SE-21: `insul_eff` = `INSUL_MC` x min(`integrity_bp`, `SUIT_LEAK_HP`) / `SUIT_LEAK_HP`), and `COLD_ROOM_DRIFT` also prints the -15000 projection |
| `T` | int mC | -30000 to -20000 | authored `T0` (`a_machine` -22000) | Room temperature |
| `TL_GAIN_MIN`, `TL_GAIN_MAX`, `TL_DEPTH_CAP_MC`, `TL_CRITICAL`, `TL_MAX` | int | 8 / 40 / 20000 / 4000 / 12000 | Suit knobs (SE D.4) | Exposure gain per run and the two thresholds |
| `S_ext` | int tiles | 13 for `a_machine` | DA-7 `exposed_tiles` | Hull tiles exposed |
| `K_HULL` | int | 25 | LSG D.7 | Hull conductance |
| `depth_tiles` | int | 7 for `a_machine` | C.2 | Door to far wall |

**Output Range:** 10 Graph runs per second. At `T0` the room is CRITICAL after 364 runs (36.4 s) and FAILED after 1,091 (109.1 s); as the room cools toward its equilibrium the gain rises and the margins shrink.
**Example:** `a_machine` `T0` -22000: e 2000, gain 8 + 32 x 2000 / 20000 = 11, `runs_critical` ceil(4000 / 11) = 364, `runs_failed` ceil(12000 / 11) = 1,091. Equilibrium: -30000 + ceil(100000 / 325) = -30000 + 308 = -29692 mC, far below -20000 (SE-21 satisfied, `[derived; Graph owner to confirm with LSG D.7, OQ-DA-4]`); there e = 9692, gain 8 + 32 x 9692 / 20000 = 23, `runs_critical` 174 (17.4 s). Crossing: 7 x 48 / 1 = 336 ticks = 5.6 s carrying, 3.1 times shorter than the worst-case CRITICAL time (reported and reviewed, not a lint threshold). Crossing is safe and lingering is lethal: the patch channel inside is the dangerous act (DA-28).

### D.11 Dead-fault back-date

The `dead_fault_back_date` formula is defined as:

`fail_since_step = -(DRIED_AFTER_STEPS + DRY_MARGIN_STEPS)` ; `since_steps(step_no) = step_no - fail_since_step` ; `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED iff -fail_since_step < DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` for every authored subject at `hp` <= `HP_FAIL` on an unserved branch

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` | int steps | 12000 `[PLACEHOLDER: Failure & Trail]` (registry range 7500 to 36000) | registry; FT:788 | Steps after which a trail's final stop is drawn |
| `DRY_MARGIN_STEPS` | int steps | 600 to 26000 (2400) | Authoring knob | Margin so a modest F&T retune does not re-wet the fault |
| `fail_since_step` | signed int steps | -14400 at the placeholder | Graph node field (LS-3, FT:317); the sign is an ASK to the Graph owner (OQ-DA-1) | The step the subject notionally failed at |

**Output Range:** `since_steps` is 14,400 at step 0, above the 12,000 threshold by 2,400. If F&T raises `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` above 14,400 the lint fails every dead fault, which is the intended tripwire; back-dating to -(36000 + 2400) = -38400 would cover the whole registered range but is outside it as a trail input (EC below).
A LIVE fault may also be back-dated (CLT-LEAK, -60) so that its trail age at wake matches the fixture; it is not on the `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED` scope list; the sign ask OQ-DA-1 covers it too.
**Example:** `pa_frozen`, `j_a_main`, `pb_leak` and `seg_b_breach` are each authored at `fail_since_step` -14400: at step 0 `since_steps` = 14,400, at least 12,000, pass. At 18,000 the lint reports four ERRORs naming the four records, the owner (Failure & Trail) and both numbers; no auto-fix.

### D.12 Fault-glow cascade bound

The `fault_glow_cascade_bound` formula is defined as:

`glow_bound(F_body) = ceil(1.25 x F_body) = floor((125 x F_body + 99) / 100)` ; `FAULT_GLOW_BOUND iff glow_bound(F_body) > MAX_FAULT_GLOW`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `F_body` | int | 0 to 8 | the declared cascade hops (DA-15) | Most faults with a glow row active at once on one body, counting woken dead faults and declared cascade hops |
| `MAX_FAULT_GLOW` | int | 10 `[PLACEHOLDER: Emitter]` | registry | Cap on simultaneous fault-glow emitters |
| 1.25 | fixed ratio | 125 / 100 | EM D.15 I-CAPS (c) | Cascade allowance |

**Output Range:** 0 at `F_body` 0 to 10 at `F_body` 8 (the cap is reached exactly at 8); `F_body` 9 gives 12 and fails.
**Example:** welded body (home + B): O2-SHIP, PWR-OVL, FUEL-DRIP, O2-ROOM and B's SCRAM rim row = 5 gives (625 + 99) / 100 = 7; after V12 wakes B's dead coolant fault the worst case is 6, giving 8. Both at or under 10 (margin 2). Derelict A alone: the broken-breaker arc (1 glow row; STARVE and FREEZE have none) gives 2 (ceil(1.25) = 2). The welded-body worst case (6 rows, bound 8) counts B's woken coolant fault conservatively: a coolant LEAK has no glow row (EM-17), so that figure is an overcount that breaches no cap. The list in DA-15 is the "declared cascade hops" the Emitter asks for.

### D.13 Weld offset (Layout D.6)

The `weld_offset` formula is defined as:

`offset_b = offset_a + pos_a - pos_b` ; `pos = (line_px, start_px)` for an E or W facing ; `LATTICE_OK iff offset_b.x mod 24 = 0 and offset_b.y mod 24 = 0` ; `GEOM_OVERLAP iff any rect of B's rects_in (rooms, wall runs, jambs, props, hatches) overlaps any rect of the home body after the offset` (Layout LY-14, LY D.6; cited, not restated)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `offset_a` | int px pair | (0, 0) | the home body's section offset | The host section is the origin |
| `pos_a` | int px pair | (720, 672) | `sock_airlock_e` (DA-10) | Host socket position |
| `pos_b` | int px pair | (24, 288) | `sock_b_w` (DA-10) | Mate socket position |

**Output Range:** a pair of multiples of 24 (the 24 px lattice, LY-3); non-multiples are a Layout ERROR.
**Example:** (0, 0) + (720, 672) - (24, 288) = **(696, 384)**, both multiples of 24 (29 x 24 and 16 x 24). B's west hull wall x [24, 48) lands at [720, 744), abutting the home east wall [696, 720); B's hatch span y [288, 384) lands at [672, 768), identical to the home socket span. `b_dock` occupies x [744, 936), y [576, 816), clear of the home `airlock` (x below 696) and of `spine_corridor` (x below 672, y below 552). Wall strips: `spine_corridor` is 13 tiles wide (x 48 to 672), so the home corridor's east hull wall is x [672, 696); it and B's west dock wall x [720, 744) are separated by the airlock's east wall [696, 720), so no rect pair overlaps: no `GEOM_OVERLAP`. The hulls abut along x 720 for the airlock's east wall (y [576, 816), containing the socket span [672, 768)); Layout allows contact outside the socket and counts it with its `HULL_CONTACT_OUTSIDE_SOCKET` diagnostic (LY Edge Cases). (The corridor was shortened from 14 tiles to 13 so that no wall strip of the two bodies shares a cell; D.1, D.3 and D.8 carry the re-run figures.)

### D.14 Leaky-kind wear clock

The `leaky_kind_wear_clock` formula is defined as:

`dp = floor(W_base x rate_pm x stress_pm / 10^6)` bp per wear period ; `periods_to(hp0, thr) = ceil((hp0 - thr) / dp)` ; `T_s = periods x WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS / 10` seconds of **loaded** section time ; `T_destroy = periods_to(hp0, 0)`

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `W_base` | int bp per 100 steps | 16 (`LEAK_WEAR_BP_PER_100`), 50 (`HULL_FATIGUE_BP_PER_100`) | LSG D.9 | The wear terms that apply to the subject (here the leak term) |
| `rate_pm` | int permille | 1000 HOME; 2000 DERELICT | LSG D.9 derived line = 10^6 / `DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` 500 | Section time multiplier |
| `stress_pm` | int permille | 1000 (2000 under `BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT`) | LSG D.9 | Not stressed in authored content |
| `WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS` | int steps | 100 | LSG D.9 | Wear lumps every 10 s |
| `hp0`, `thr` | int bp | 0 to 3999 for a LEAK-mode subject; `HP_FAIL` 3000 or 0 | DA-17 and DA-15 tables | Authored `hp`; threshold FAILED or DESTROYED |

**Output Range:** `dp` is 16 HOME and 32 DERELICT for a leak. For a LEAK-mode subject at `hp0` 3999, 250 periods (41.7 minutes) to DESTROYED on HOME and 125 periods (20.8 minutes) on a DERELICT.
**Example (literal LSG D.9 reading, which binds content until the Graph owner answers ASK-G1, OQ-DA-18):**
